Chapter 2 IAM

2.1 IAM 101

  AWS IAM Features
    https://aws.amazon.com/iam/features/


4.1 Identity Access Management 101
  Resources:
   https://docs.aws.amazon.com/IAM/latest/UserGuide/access_policies_job-functions.html

  - What is IAM
    - IAM allows you to manage user and their level of access to the AWS console
      - Create user and grant permissions to those users
      - Create groups and roles
      - Control acces to AWS resources

  - key features of IAM
    - centralized control of your AWS account
    - Shared access to your AWS account
    - Granular permissions
    - identity federation (including Active Directory, Facebook, Linkedin, etc)
      - user could use their same login to their PC to login to AWS
    - Multifactor Authentification
    - Provide temporary access for users/devices and services where necessary
    - allows you to set up your own password rotation policy
    - integrates with many different AWS services
    - Support PCI DSS (Payment Card Industry Data Security Standard) Compliance
       - compliant framework for taking credit card details


  - Key terminology for IAM
    - Users - end users such as people, employees, etc.
    - Groups - A collection of users. Each user in the group will inherit the permissions of the group
             - functions such as administrator, developers, etc - contains users
    - Roles - you create roles then assign them to AWS resources
       - Internal usage within AWS - allow one resource access another resource
    - Policies -  made up of documents called policy documents.
         - in JSON format and they give permissions as to what a user/group//role is able to do
         - policies can be attached to a user, group, or role

2.2 IAM Demo

  Create a new AWS Account:
    https://repost.aws/knowledge-center/create-and-activate-aws-account

  Create a billing Alarm
    https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonCloudWatch/latest/monitoring/monitor_estimated_charges_with_cloudwatch.html

  Create a Free Tier Billing Alert
    https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2017/12/aws-free-tier-usage-alerts-automatically-notify-you-when-you-are-forecasted-to-exceed-your-aws-service-usage-limits/

     Steps
       - Set up Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA)
       - Create a User
       - Create a Group
       - Create a Role
       - Exam Tips

     Prerequite
       - Use your own AWS account for this lesson

         -> AWS Console -> IAM -> Add MFA (next to root account) -> Assign MFA ->
              Device Name: MyGoggleMFA, select "Authenticator App" -> Next ->
              1. install compatible App (e.g. Google Authenticator)
              2. "Show QR Code"
                - scan QR code with App

             -> refresh IAM dashboard -> it now shows root user has MFA
              3. enter 2 consective codes from your MFA device

        # Create user
          -> Users -> Add user -> User Name: fred, select "Provide access to ... Console",
           select "autogenerated password, select "Users must create a new password ..." -> Next
           Set Permissions:  "Add User to Group", Create Group, User Group Name: sysadmin,
           Policy Name: AdministratorAccess -> Create User Group
           -> select add user "sysadmin" group -> Review -> Create User


           AdministratorAccess Policy:
           {
               "Version": "2012-10-17",
               "Statement": [
                   {
                       "Effect": "Allow",
                       "Action": "*",
                       "Resource": "*"
                   }
               ]
           }

           # create next user, Robbie, in sysadmin group
           # create dev group with S3ReadOnlyAccess
           # remove Robbie fro sysadmin group and add to dev group
           # add DynamoDBReadOnlyAccess directly to Robbie

           # Change Password Policy
           -> IAM -> Account Settings -> Password Policy -> Edit -> Custom ->
             Change [min] password length: from 8 to 12
             Add Password Strenth options
               Password Strength
                 x Require at least one uppercase letter from the Latin alphabet (A-Z)
                 x Require at least one lowercase letter from the Latin alphabet (a-z)
                 x Require at least one number
                 x Require at least one non-alphanumeric character ( ! @ # $ % ^ & * ( ) _ + - = [ ] {} | ' )
               Other requirements
                 x Turn on password expiration
                   - Expire password in "90" days
                   Password expiration requires administrator reset
                   Allow users to change their own password
                   Prevent password reuse
               -> Save Changes -> Set custom

             # Create Role
                 -> IAM -> Roles -> Create Role
                    -> AWS Service -> EC2 -> Next -> Add Permission: Policy Name: AmazonS3FullAccess -> Next
                      Role Details: Role Name: EC2S3FullAccess -> Create Role
   Exam Tips
      Users
        - End Users - think people
      Groups
        - A collection of users under one set of permissions
      Roles
        - can create roles and can then assign them to users [Web Identity users, SAML 2.0 Federation
          users], AWS Accounts, applications, and services to give them access to AWS resources

      - IAM is universal (global)
      - The "root account" is simply the account created when first setup your AWS account.
        It has complete Admin access
      - New users have no permissions when first created
      - Optionally, New user are assigned Access Key ID & Secret Access Keys when 1st created
        for programmatic access
      - only get to view new users password, Access Key, & Secret Access Key once so save
        them in a secure location, else if lost, you have to regenerate them

      - Always setup Multifactor Authentication on your root account
      - can create and customize your own password rotation policy

2.4 Testing IAM Policy Permissions

  Testing IAM Policies with IAM policy simulator
    https://docs.aws.amazon.com/IAM/latest/UserGuide/access_policies_testing-policies.html

   IAM Policy Simulator
     - test the effects of IAM Policy before committing them to production
     - Validate that the policy works as expected
     - test policies alread attached to existing users
       - great for troubleshooting an issue which you suspect is IAM related
     - to access, when logged in to AWS console go to:
             http://policysim.aws.amazon.com

     To use:
             http://policysim.aws.amazon.com
             -> Select User [or Groups, or Roles]
                -> Under "Policy Simulator" -> Select Service
                   -> Select Action(s)
                     -> Run Simulator
                       -> reports allow or denied
                         -> if allowed, you can click "Show Statement" to see policy statement
                            allowing action
  Exam Tips

   IAM Policy Simulator
     - test IAM permissions before you commit them to production
     - Validate that the policy works as expected
     - test policies already attached to existing users
       - great for troubleshooting an issue which you suspect is IAM related
     - to access, when logged in to AWS console go to:
             http://policysim.aws.amazon.com

2.5 IAM 101 Summary

  Exam Tips
    IAM 101 - Summary
      Users
        - End Uses - think people
      Groups
        - A collection of users under one set of permissions
      Roles
        - can create roles and can then assign them to users [Web Identity users, SAML 2.0 Federation
          users], AWS Accounts, applications, and services to give them access to AWS resources

       Policies:
         Example policy JSON document
           - JSON document written as a series of Key Value pairs

           AdministratorAccess Policy:
           {
               "Version": "2012-10-17",
               "Statement": [
                   {
                       "Effect": "Allow",
                       "Action": "*",
                       "Resource": "*"
                   }
               ]
           }


      - IAM is universal (global), not regional
      - The "root account" is simply the account created when first setup your AWS account.
         - It has complete Admin access
         - The account is the email address that you used to create the account
         - should not be using root account for day to day activities, but instead create IAM user accounts
         - IAM accounts should have the minimum privileges to due their jobs
      - New users have no permissions when first created

    IAM access Keys - Exam Tips
      Access keys
        - Optionally, New user are assigned Access Key ID & Secret Access Keys when 1st created
          for programmatic access
        - only get to view new users password, Access Key, & Secret Access Key once so save
          them in a secure location, else if lost, you have to regenerate them
           - Note: Access key can be viewed under IAM users "name" -> "security credentials" tab,
             but "Secret Access key can only be view when generated (via CSV download file)
        - Not for console access
          - these are not the same as a passwords, and cannot uses the access key ID or secret access
            key to log in to the AWS Management Console
        - Programmatic Access
          - you can use access keys to access AWS via the API and command line

      Use MFA
        - Always setup Multifactor Authentication on your root account
      Password Rotation
        - can create and customize your own password rotation policy

Chapter 3 Beginner's Guide to EC2

---------------------------------------------------
3.1 Introducing EC2

 Introducting Elastic Compute Cloud (EC2)
    - secure, resizable compute capacity in the Cloud
    - VM hosted in AWS
    - only pay for what you use
    - the capacity you want when you need it
    - wait minutes, not months

  On-premise infrastructure
    - long-term investments, 3 - 5 years
    - can take 3 months or longer for new compute in your own data centers to come online

Exam Tips

    EC2 Overview
      - EC2 is like a VM, hosted in AWS instead of your own data center
      - select the capacity your need right now
      - grow and shrink when you need
      - pay for what you use
      - to provision, wait for minutes, not months to access

---------------------------------------------------
3.2 EC2 Pricing

  AWS Pricing Calculator
    https://calculator.aws/#/

  AWS Whitepaper: Amazon EC2 Reserved Instances and Other AWS Reservation Models
    -> downloaded "cost-optimization-reservation-models.pdf" PDF

  Scheduled Reserved Instances
    https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AWSEC2/latest/UserGuide/ec2-scheduled-instances.html
    Note: You cannot purchase Scheduled Reserved Instances at this time. AWS does not have any capacity
          available for Scheduled Reserved Instances or any plans to make it available in the future.
          To reserve capacity, use On-Demand Capacity Reservations instead.
          For discounted rates, use Savings Plans.

  EC2 pricing options
    on-demand instances
      - pay by the hour or second depending on the type of instance you run
    reserved instances
      - reserve capacity for 1 to 3 years
      - up to 72% discount on the hourly charge
      - the longer the contract and the more you pay up front, the greater the discount
      - not only EC2, but also include serverless technologies like lambda and Fargate
      - reserved instances are region based
    spot instances
      - purchase unused capacity at a discount of up to 90%
      - prices fluctuate with supply and demand
      - when price exceeds your max price to pay for the instance, then the instance will be
        terminated or hypernated depending on the options you selected
    dedicated hosts
      - a physical EC2 server dedicated for your use
      - the most expensive option
      - use when you have multi-tenant restriction or software license tied to physical hardware
    dedicated instance
      - Dedicated Instances are EC2 instances that run on hardware that's dedicated to a single customer
      - Dedicated Instances might share hardware with other instances from the same AWS account that are not Dedicated Instances

  EC2 Use Cases
    on-demand instances
      - Flexible: low cost and flexible
      - short term: applications with short term, spikey, or unpredictable workloads that
        cannot be interrupted
      - Testing the water:
    reserved instances  (RI)
      - Predictable Usages: applications with steady state or predictable usage
      - Specific Capacity Requirements
      - Pay up front - pay upfront to reduce the total computing costs even further
      - reserved instances operate at a regional level
      - Standard RIs:
         - up to 72% off the on-demand prices
         - cannot change your reserve instance types
      - Convertible RI:
         - up to 54% off the on-demand prices.
         - Has option to change to a different RI type of equal or greater value
      - Scheduled RIs:
         - launched with the time window you define
         - match your capacity reservation to predictable recurring schedule that only requires a
           fraction of a day, week, or month
         - Note: No longer be supported according to:
            https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AWSEC2/latest/UserGuide/ec2-scheduled-instances.html
    spot instances
      - Flexible: applications that have flexible start and end times
      - Cost Sentive: applications that are only feasible at very low compute prices
      - Urgent Capacity: users with an urgent need for large amounts of additional computing capacity
      - examples: image rendering, genomic sequencing, algorithmic trading engines
    dedicated hosts
      - Compliance
        - regulatory requirements that may not support multi-tenant virtualization
      - Licensing
        - licensing that does not support multi-tenancy or cloud deployments
      - on-demand
         - dedicated hosts can be purchased on-demand
      - reserved
         - can be purchased as a reservation for up to 70% off the on-demand price

  Saving Plans
    Flexible Pricing
      - offering of flexible pricing models for up to 72% savings on compute usage
        regardless of instance type or Region
    Lower Prices
      - lower prices on EC2 instances regardless of instance family, size, OS,
        tenancy, or regions
    Variety
      - Savings can also apply to AWS Lambda and AWS Fargate usage
    SageMaker
      - SageMaker plans for lowering SageMaker instance pricing
      Note: Amazon SageMaker is a managed service in the AWS public cloud. It provides
            the tools to build, train and deploy machine learning (ML) models for predictive
            analytics applications.
    Commitments
      - Savings are provided as a trade for long-term commitments
      - there are one-year or 3-year pricing options
    Pricing Plan Options
      - Choose from All Upfront, Partial upfront, or no upfront

  Saving Plans Types
    Compute Savings
      - most flexible
      - applies to any EC2 compute, Lambda, or fargate usage
      - up to 66% savings on compute on-demand pricing
    EC2 instance Savings
      - Applies only to EC2 instances of a specific instance family in a specific Region
      - up to 72% savings
    SageMaker Savings
      - Applies to SageMaker instances regardless of instance family or sizing
      - any region and any component
      - up to 64% savings

  Using and Applying Saving Plans
    1. View recommendations within your AWS billing console
    2. Recommendations are automatically calculated to make purchasing easier
    3. Add to cart and purchase directly within your AWS account
    4. Apply to usage rates after Reserved Instances are applied and exhausted
    5. Consolidated Billing Family: Applied to the account owner first, then can be
       spread to others if you e

   AWS Calculator
      https://calculator.aws
        - use to estimate AWS cost

  Exam Tips

    EC2 pricing options
      on-demand instances
        - pay by the hour or second depending on the type of instance you run
        - great for flexiblity
      reserved instances
        - reserve capacity for 1 to 3 years
        - up to 72% discount on the hourly charge
        - the long the contract and the more you pay up front, the greater the discount
        - great if you have known, fixed requirements
      spot instances
        - purchase unused capacity at a discount of up to 90%
        - prices fluctuate with supply and demand
        - great for applications with flexible start and end times
        - If EC2 interrupts your Spot Instance in the first hour, before a full hour of running time,
          you're not charged for the partial hour used.
        - when price exceeds your max price to pay for the instance, then the instance will be
          terminated or hypernated depending on the options you selected
      dedicated hosts
        - a physical EC2 server dedicated for your use
        - the most expensive option
        - great if you have server-bound licenses to reuse or compliance requirements

---------------------------------------------------
3.3 Exploring EC2 Instance Types

 EC2 Instance Types:
   https://aws.amazon.com/ec2/instance-types/

  Instance Type Families:
     General Purpose
      - provide a balance of compute, memory and networking resources, and can be used for a variety of
        diverse workloads.
      - ideal for applications that use these resources in equal proportions such as web servers and code
        repositories.
     Compute Optimized
       -  ideal for compute bound applications that benefit from high performance processors.
       - Instances belonging to this category are well suited for batch processing workloads, media transcoding,
         high performance web servers, high performance computing (HPC), scientific modeling, dedicated gaming
         servers and ad server engines, machine learning inference and other compute intensive applications.
     Memory Optimized
       - designed to deliver fast performance for workloads that process large data sets in memory.
     Accelerated Computing
       - use hardware accelerators, or co-processors (e.g. GPUs), to perform functions, such as floating point
         number calculations, graphics processing, or data pattern matching, more efficiently than is possible
         in software running on CPUs.
       - includes F1 instance type with FPGAs
       - includes VT1 instance type that are used for Real-time video transcoding
     Storage Optimized
       - designed for workloads that require high, sequential read and write access to very large data sets on
         local storage.
       - optimized to deliver tens of thousands of low-latency, random I/O operations per second (IOPS) to
         applications.
     HPC Optimized
       - purpose built to offer the best price performance for running HPC workloads at scale on AWS.
       - ideal for applications that benefit from high-performance processors such as large, complex simulations
         and deep learning workloads.

  Exam Tips

    Instance Type
      - determines the hardware of the host computer
      - each instance type offers different compute, memory, and storage capabilities
      - grouped in Instance families
      - select an instance type based on the requirements of your application

---------------------------------------------------
3.4 Launching an EC2 Instance

# create ec2 webserver instance

-> Console -> EC2 -> Name: myWebServer, AMI: Amazon Linux 2, instance type: t2.micro,
   create new key pair (used xxx_xxxyyyyykey),
   Network: VPC: default VPC,  subnet: default for us-east-1a,  Auto-assign public IP: enable,
   create security Group: mywebdmz, SG Rules: ssh anywhere, HTTP anywhere;
   Storage: 8 GiB gp3 (default)
   -> Launch Instance

   # linux prompt
   mv <PEM> ~/.ssh
   chmod 400 ~/.ssh/<PEM>
   ssh -i ~/.ssh/xxx_xxxyyyyykey.pem ec2-user@<publicIP>
   sudo su
   yum update -y
   # install apache webserver
   you install httpd -y
   # start Apache Webserver
   systemctl start httpd
   systemctl enable httpd
   systemctl status httpd
   # create initial webpage
   cd /var/www/html
   # create index.html
   vi index.html
   <html><body><h1>Hello Cloud Gurus !</h1></body></html>

   # in Web Browser, go to <public IP>

   # terminate instance

             Private key file for authentication: <KEY>.ppk
         go back to "session" window:
            Save Sessions: ec2-user@<publicIP>
                    -> SAVE

               -> connects to ec2 (did not work)

3.7 Understanding EBS Volumes

  EBS Volume Documentation
    https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AWSEC2/latest/UserGuide/ebs-volume-types.html

  EBS Volume Types
    https://aws.amazon.com/ebs/volume-types/


  Elastic Block Store Volumes
    - storage volumes that you can attach to your EC2 instances
    - different types of EBS volumes and Use cases for each type
    - virtual disk in the cloud

  Elastic Block Store Volumes Useages:
    - use that the same way you would use any system disk
    - to create a file system
    - to Run a database
    - to run an operating system
    - to store data
    - to store application

  Mission Critical
    - Production workloads
       - designed for mission-critical workloads
    - Highly available
      - automatically replicated within a single AZ to protect against HW failures
    - scalable
      - dynamically increase capacity and change the volume type with no downtime or
        performance impact to your live systems

  EBS Volume Types: Solid State Disk
    General Purpose SSD (gp2)
      - balance of price and performance
      - 3 IOPS per GiB, up to 16K IOPS per volume
      - gp2 volumes smaller than 1 TB can burst up to 3K IOPS
      - Good for boot volumes or development and test applications that are not latency sensitive
    General Purpose SSD (gp3)
      - balance of price and performance
      - Predicatable 3K IOPS baseline performance and 125 Mb/s regardless of volume size (1 GB - 16 TB)
      - customers  looking for higher performance can scale up to 16K IOPS and 1000 Mb/s for additional fee
      - 20% cheaper than gp2
      - Good for boot volumes or development and test applications that are not latency sensitive
      - Ideal for applications that require high performance at a low cost, such as MySQL, Cassandra,
        virtual desktops, and Hadoop analytics
      - top performance of gp3 is 4 times faster than max throughput of gp2
    Provisioned IOPS SSD (io1)
       - legacy high performance option and the most expensive
       - 50 IOPS per GB, up to 64K IOPS per volume
       - use if you need more than 16K IOPS
       - used for I/O-intensive applications, large databases, and latency-sensitive workloads
    Provisioned IOPS SSD (io2)
       - 500 IOPS per GB, up to 64K IOPS
       - 99.999% durability instead of up to 99.9%
       - higher durability and more IOPS than io1
       - io2 is the same price as io1
       - used for I/O-intensive applications, large databases, and latency-sensitive workloads.
         Applications that need high levels of durability
    Provisioned IOPS SSD (io2 Block Express)
       - SAN (Storage Area Network) in the cloud
       - highest performance, sub-millsecond latency
       - underline hardware using EBS block express architecture
       - 4x throughput, IOPs, and capacity of regular io2 volumes
       - up to 64 TB, 256K IOPS per volume
       - 99.999 durability
       - latest generation - available only with some instance types and in some regions
       - same durability as io2 and more IOPS than io2
       - io2 Block Express is the same price as io2
       - great for the largest, most critical, high-performance applications like SAP HANA, Oracle,
         Microsoft SQL Server, and IBM DB2

  EBS Volume Types: Hard Disk Drives (Mb/s-Intensive)
    Throughput Optimized HDD (st1)
      - low-cost HDD volume
      - baseline throuphput of 40 Mb/s per TB
      - Ability to burst up to 250 Mb/s per TB
      - max throughput of 500 Mb/s per volume
      - Used for Frequently accessed, throughput-intensive workloads
      - use cases: big data, data warehousing, ETL (Extract/Tranform/Load), and log processing
      - cost-effective way to store mountains of data
      - cannot be a boot volume
    Cold HDD (sc1)
      - low cost options
      - baseline throughput of 12 Mb/s per TB
      - Ability to burst up to 80 Mb/s per TB
      - max throughput of 250 Mb/s per volume
      - a good choice for colder data requiring fewer scans per day
      - good for applications that need the lowest cost and performance is not a factor
      - cannot be a boot volume

  IOPs vs Throughput
    IOPS
      - measures the number of read and write operations per second
      - important metric for quick transactions, low-latency apps, and transactional workloads
      - ability to action reads and writes very quickly
      - for IOPS, choose provisioned IOPS SSD (io1 or io2)
    Throughput
      - measures the number of bits read or written per sec (Mb/s)
      - important metric for large datasets, large I/O sizes, complex queries
      - the ability to deal with large datasets
      - For thoughput, Choose Throughput Optimized HDD (st1)

  Note:
    Amazon Data Lifecycle Manager
      - use to automate the creation, retention, and deletion of EBS snapshots and EBS-backed AMIs
      - When combined with the monitoring features of CloudWatch Events and AWS CloudTrail, Amazon Data Lifecycle
        Manager provides a complete backup solution for Amazon EC2 instances and individual EBS volumes at no additional cost.
      - it helps you:
        - Protect valuable data by enforcing a regular backup schedule.
        - Create standardized AMIs that can be refreshed at regular intervals.
        - Retain backups as required by auditors or internal compliance.
        - Reduce storage costs by deleting outdated backups.
        - Create disaster recovery backup policies that back up data to isolated accounts.

  Exam Tips

    EBS volume type differences
      https://aws.amazon.com/premiumsupport/knowledge-center/ebs-volume-type-differences/

    EBS SSD Volume types
      Note: if you want more than 16K IOPS, then io1/io2
      General Purpose SSD (gp2)
        - suitable for boot disks and general applications
        - 16K IOPS per volume
        - up to 99.9 durability
        - Good for boot volumes or development and test applications that are not latency sensitive
      General Purpose SSD (gp3)
        - suitable for high perfomance applications
        - Predicatable 3K IOPS baseline perfomrance and 125 Mb/s regardless of volume size
        - up to 99.9 durability
        - 20% cheaper than gp2
      Provisioned IOPS SSD (io1)
        - suitable for OLTP (On-Line Transaction Processing) and latency-sensitive applications
        - 4K MB/s throughput per volume, up to 64K IOPS per volume
        - 50 IOPS per GiB
        - use if you need more than 16K IOPS
        - up to 99.9 durability
        - used for High perfomance and most expensive
      Provisioned IOPS SSD (io2)
        - suitable for OLTP (On-Line Transaction Processing) and latency-sensitive applications
        - 500 IOPS per GiB, up to 64K IOPS
        - 99.999 durability instead of up to 99.9
        - same [base?] price for io2 as io1
      Provisioned IOPS SSD (io2 block express)
        - for the largest, most critical, high-performance applications like SAP HANA, Oracle,
          Microsoft SQL Server, and IBM DB2
        - highest performance, sub-millsecond latency
        - up to 64 TB, 256K IOPS per volume
        - 99.999 durability
        - SAN (Storage Area Network) in the cloud
        - latest generation - available only with some instance types and in some regions
        - same durability as io2 and more IOPS than io2
        - io2 Block Express is the same price as io2

    EBS HDD Volume types
      Throughput Optimized HDD (st1)
        - use cases: big data, data warehousing, ETL (Extract/Tranform/Load), and log processing
        - max throughput of 500 Mb/s per volume
        - cannot be a boot volume
      Cold HDD (sc1)
        - max throughput of 250 Mb/s per volume
        - used for less frequently accessed data
        - low cost options
        - cannot be a boot volume
        - up to 99.9 durability

    EBS Volume comparison table:
      https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AWSEC2/latest/UserGuide/ebs-volume-types.html

---------------------------------------------------
3.8 Create EBS Volume - Demo

   # create EC2 instance

   # attach a 2nd volume to the EC2 instance
    - must create volume in same AZ as your instance

     -> console -> EC2 -> EBS Volumes -> Create Volume:
       Volume type: gp3, size: 100 GiB, AZ: <same as EC2>
       -> create volume

       # wait till volume is created
       -> select volume -> Action -> Attach Volume -> select EC2 instance
           shows default dev name (which you can change)
         -> Attach volume

    Notes:
      - if encryption is set at an account level, then you will only be able to create encrypted volumes
      - If you use an encrypted snapshot, the volume will be encrypted


  Exam Tips:

    Default Encryption
      - if encryption by default is set on your account by your account admin, you cannot create
        unecrypted volumes
    Encrypted Snapshots
      - If you create an EBS volume from an encrypted snapshot, then you will get an encrypted volume
    Unencrypted Snapshots
      - If you create an EBS volume from an unencrypted snapshot, then encryption is only optional if
        default encryption has not been set at account level by your account admin

3.9 Exploring Elastic Load Balancers

  ELB features compared:
    https://aws.amazon.com/elasticloadbalancing/features/
  Gateway Load Balancer
    https://aws.amazon.com/elasticloadbalancing/gateway-load-balancer/
  AWS Elastic Load Balancing: Support for SSL Termination
    https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/aws/elastic-load-balancer-support-for-ssl-termination/
  Create an HTTPS Listener for your Application Load Balancer
    https://docs.aws.amazon.com/elasticloadbalancing/latest/application/create-https-listener.html
  Elastic Load Balancer Troubleshooting
    https://docs.aws.amazon.com/elasticloadbalancing/latest/application/load-balancer-troubleshooting.html

  What is Elastic Load Balancing
    - automatically distributes incoming application traffic across multiple targets, such as
      EC2 instances.
    - this can be done across multiple AZs

  4 Types of Load Balancers
     Application Load Balancer
       - bested suited for load balancing of HTTP and HTTPS traffic
       - operates at Layer 7 of the OSI model and are application aware (e.g. routing HTTP and HTTPS based
         routings, content routing)
       - intelligent load balancing
       - support advanced request routing to specific web servers based on the HTTP header
         - e.g. car dealership App Load Balancer can send sales, loans, and Service&Repairs requests each to
           different servers
     Network Load Balancer
       - TCP and high performnace
       - operates at the transport layer (layer 4)
       - used for load balancing TCP traffic where extreme performance is required
       - are capable of handling millions of requests per sec, while maintaining ultra-low latenceis
       - performance load balancer
       - the most expensive option
       - The static IP address that's assigned to a Network Load Balancer doesn't change and provides
         a fixed entry point for your Application Load Balancer.
     Classic Load Balancer
       - legacy load balancers
       - handles HTTP/HTTPS (layer 7) and TCP (layer 4) traffic
       - can load HTTP/HTTPS applications and use layer-7 specific features, such a X-Forwarded and
         stickly seessions
       - Classic/Test/Dev Load Balancer
     Gateway Load Balancer
       - allows you to load balance workloads for 3rd party virtual appliances running in AWS, such as:
          - Virtual appliances purchased using AWS Marketplace
          - Virtual firewalls from companies like Fortinet, Palo Alto, Juniper, Cisco
          - IDS/IPS systems from companies like CheckPoint, Trend Micro, etc


  Notes:
    https://www.okta.com/identity-101/ids-vs-ips/
    Intrusion detection system (IDS):
      - monitors traffic on your network, analyzes that traffic for signatures matching known attacks, and when
        something suspicious happens, you're alerted. In the meantime, the traffic keeps flowing.
    Intrusion Prevention System (IPS):
      - also monitors traffic. But when something unusual happens, the traffic stops altogether until you
        investigate and decide to open the floodgates again.

  The  OSI (Open Systems Interconnection) Model
    7 layers Model:
      - a conceptual framework which describes the functions of a network
      - beginning with the application layer which directly serves the end user down to the physical layer
      Layer 7 Application
        - what the end user sees, HTTP, web browers
      Layer 6 Presentation
        - data is in usable format; protocols for encryption, ssh operate at this layer
      Layer 5 Session
        - maintains connections and sessions
      Layer 4 Transport
        - transmits data using TCP and UDP
      Layer 3 Network
        - logically routes packets, based on IP addresses
      Layer 2 Data Link
        - physically transmits data based on MAC addresses
      Layer 1 Physical
        - transmits bits and bytes over physical devices


  X-Forwarded-For Header
     - identify the originating IP address of a client connecting through a Load balancer
     - when traffic is sent from a LB, the server access logs contain the IP address of the
       proxy or LB only
     - to see the original IP address of the client, the X-Forwarded-For requset header is used
     - The X-Forwarded-For request header may contain multiple IP addresses that are comma separated.
       The left-most address is the client IP where the request was first made. This is followed by any
       subsequent proxy identifiers, in a chain.

  X-Forwarded-For header e.g.:
     user         -> load balancer    -> EC2
     124.12.3.231    10.0.0.23        see the request from 10.0.0.23
                                      X-Forwarded-For header has: 124.12.3.231

  Common Load Balancer Errors:
     Error 504 Gateway Timeout
       - The  target failed to respond
       - this could be either at the web server layer or database layer
       - 1st check the application
         - the ELB could not establish a connection to the target, e.g., the webserver, database, or
           lambda function
           - your application is having issues
           - identify where the application is failing and fix the problem


  ELB Exam Tips

  4 Types of Load Balancers
     Application Load Balancer
       - best suited for load balancing of HTTP and HTTPS traffic
       - operates at Layer 7 and are application aware
       - intelligent load balancing
     Network Load Balancer
       - operates at the transport layer (layer 4)
       - are capable of handling millions of requests per sec, while maintaining ultra-low latenceis
       - high performance load balancer for TCP traffic
       - can be assigned a static IP address (note: ALB cannot be assigned a static IP address)
     Classic Load Balancer
       - legacy load balancers
       - can load HTTP/HTTPS applications and use layer-7 specific features, such a X-Forwarded and
         stickly seessions
       - Classic/Test/Dev Load Balancer
     Gateway Load Balancer
       - allows you to load balance workloads for 3rd party virtual appliances running in AWS, such as:
          - Virtual appliances purchased using AWS Marketplace
          - Virtual firewalls from companies like Fortinet, Palo Alto, Juniper, Cisco
          - IDS/IPS systems from companies like CheckPoint, Trend Micro, etc

  X-Forwarded-For
    - if you need the IPv4 address of your end user, look for the X-Forwarded header

---------------------------------------------------

   OSI (Open Systems Interconnection) Model
    https://www.imperva.com/learn/application-security/osi-model/

    - describes the 7 layers that computer systems use to communicate over a network
    - the internet is not based on OSI, but on the simplier TCP/IP model, but OSI is widely used

    7 layers Model:
      - a conceptual framework which describes the functions of a network
      7 Application Layer
        - Human-computer interaction layer where applications can access the network services
        - used by web browsers and email clients
        - application protocols include HTTP, FTP, Simple Mail Transfer Protocol (SMTP), DNS
      6 Presentation Layer
        - Ensures that data is in usable format and is where data encryption occurs
        - defines how 2 devices should encode, encrypt, and compress data so it is received correctly
          at the other end
      5 Session Layer
        - maintains connections and is responsible for controlling ports and sessions
        - creates communication channels, called sessions, between devices
      4 Transport Layer
        - transmits data using transmission protocols including TCP and UDP
        - takes data transferred in the session layer and breaks it into "segments" on transmitting end
        - it resposible for reassemblying the segments on the receiving end
      3 Network Layer
        - decides which physical path the data will take
        - 2 main functions:
           1: breaking up segments into network packets, and reasembling the backs on the receiving end;
           2: routing packets by discovering the best path across a physical network
        - uses network addresses (typically IP addresses) to route packets
      2 Data Link Layer
        - defines the format of the data on the network
      1 Physical Layer
        - transmit raw bit stream over the physical medium
        - responsible for the physical cable or wireless connections between network nodes

---------------------------------------------------
3.10 Route 53 Demo

   Route 53
     - Amazon's DNS service
     - allows you to map a domain name that you own to:
        - EC2 instances
        - load balancers
        - S3 buckets

    Demo
      1. Launch an EC2 Instance
        - install httpd
      2. Create an Application Load Balancer
        - configure our EC2 instance as a target
      3. Configure a Route 53 Alias
        - map a friendly domain name to our Application Load Balancer so we can access our website using
          our domain name (e.g. ilovecloud.com) instead of the App LB DNS name

      A Cloud Guru Sandbox:
        - provides a Route 53 hosted zone for you to use
        - hosted Zone is a container for DNS record
        - already configured with a DNS registered domain name owned by the sandbox account
        - ec2 bootstrap script:
           #!/bin/bash
           yum update -y
           yum install httpd -y
           echo "<html><body><h1>Hello Cloud Gurus</h1></body></html>" >/var/www/html/index.html
           systemctl start httpd
           systemctl enable httpd


   -> open Cloud playground
      -> Route 53 -> Hosted Zones -> one hosted zone (DNS Name) exits
      # create ec2 instance
      -> ec2 -> launch instance -> name: mywebsever, AL2, t3.micro, no key pair,
         Network: vpc: default, subnet: us-east-1a, Auto-assign public IP: enable;
         Create SG: mywebdmz, HTTP anywhere  ;
         Advanced details: User Data:
            #!/bin/bash
            yum update -y
            yum install httpd -y
            echo "<html><body><h1>Hello Cloud Gurus</h1></body></html>" >/var/www/html/index.html
            systemctl start httpd
            systemctl enable httpd
         -> Launch Instance

         -> ec2 -> select ec2  -> copy instance id: i-035c025c11f77d163
         -> ec2  -> Load Balancers ->  Create Load Balancer -> Application load Balancer ->
           Name: myalb, Scheme: internet-facing, IP Address Type: IPv4;
           Network Mapping: VPC: default, Mappings (AZs): us-east-1a & us-east-1b (most pick 2+),
           Security Group: mywebdmz, delete default SG
           Listener: Protocol: HTTP,
           # note: create target group opens new tab
           create target group: Name: mytg,  Protocol: HTTP, VPC: default, Health Checks: Protocol: HTTP, path: /index.html
           -> Next -> Register Targets: Available Instances: select mywebserver ec2, click on "include as pending below"
              -> Create target group
               -> examine "mytg" and verify it includes your "mywebserver" ec2
            # go back to create ELB tab:
            Listeners and routing: Default Action: mytg
            -> Create Load Balancer
        # after ALB is running:
           copy ALB DNS name and place in web browser -> should connect

        # use Route 53 to map your website DNS name to ALB using A-record with Alias record
        -> Route 53 -> hosted zone -> selected hosted zone (cmcloudlab*.info)
           -> create record -> Record type: A, select "Alias" ,
             Route traffic to: Alias to Application and Classic Load Balancer
             Region: us-east-1, choose load balancer: dualstack.myalb*.us-east-1.elb.amazon.aws.com
             Routing Policy: Simple routing
             -> Create Record
         # after record create
            in web brower, go to the DNS Name: cmcloudlab1656.info


  Exam Tips:

      1. Launch an EC2 Instance
        - install httpd
      2. Create an Application Load Balancer
        - configure our EC2 instance as a target
      3. Configure a Route 53 Alias
        - map a friendly domain name to our Application Load Balancer so we can access our website using
          our domain name (e.g. ilovecloud.com) instead of the App LB DNS name
          - Used Route 53 create record to created an A-record, which is also an Alias, to map out Domain
            name to the Load Balancer's DNS address

        -> fails due to access no longer exists

     # remove bucket (must first be empty
     $  aws s3 rb  s3://patacloudguru
     remove_bucket: patacloudguru


   Exam Tips
     Least Privileges
       - Always give your users the minimum amount of access required to do their job
     Use Groups
       - create IAM groups an assign your users to groups
       - group permissions are assigned using IAM policy documents

      AWS CLI
         1. secret access key
           - you will only see it once. If you lose it, you can delete the access key ID and secret
             access key and regenerate them
           - You will need to run aws configure again
         2. Don't share key pairs
           - each developer should have their own access key ID and secret key.
         2. AWS CLI supports Linux, Windows, & MacOS

3.12 CLI Pagination

    CLI Pagination Commands
       #AWS CLI Pagination commands
       #Remember to replace <YOUR_BUCKET_NAME> with the name of your S3 bucket

       aws s3api list-objects --bucket <YOUR_BUCKET_NAME>
       aws s3api list-objects --bucket <YOUR_BUCKET_NAME> --page-size 5
       aws s3api list-objects --bucket <YOUR_BUCKET_NAME> --max-items 1

  AWS CLI Pagination
    - control the number of items included in the output when you run a CLI command
    - By default, the AWS CLI uses a page size of 1000
       - i.e. if you run "aws s3api lists-objects my_bucket - on a bucket which contains 2500
         objects, the CLI actually makes 3 API calls to S3 ..., but displays the entire output
         in one go

  AWS CLI Pagination - Errors
    - if you see errors when running list commands on a large number of resources,
      the default page size of 1000 might be too high
    - you are most likely to see a "timed out" error, because the API call has exceeded the
      maximum allowed time to fetch the required results

  Fixing AWS CLI Pagination - Errors
    1. to fix this, use the --page-size option to have the CLI request a smaller number of items
       from each API call
    2. The CLI still retrieves the full list, but performs a larger number of API calls in the
       background and retrieves a smaller number items with each call

  Fixing AWS CLI Pagination command options - Errors
    - use --page-size option to list items per background API calls
       aws s3api list-objects --bucket <myBucket> --page-size 100
    - Use the --max-items option to return fewer items in the CLI output
       aws s3api list-objects --bucket <myBucket> --max-items 100

  Exam Tips

    AWS CLI Pagination
      - if you do see errors like "timed out" or errors related to too many results being returned:
          - adjust the pagination for the CLI results to avoid errors generated by too many results
           aws s3api list-objects --bucket <myBucket> --page-size 100
      - the CLI still retrieves the full list, but it performs a larger number of API calls in
        the background and retrieves a smaller number of items with each call

  Exam Tips

   AWS Roles
     1. The Preferred Option
       - Roles are the preferred from a security perspective
     2. Avoid Hard Coding your Credentials
       - roles allow you to provide access without the use of access key ID and secret access keys
     3. Policies
       - policies control a role's permissions
     4. Policy Updates
        - you can update a policy attached to a role, and it will take immediate effect
     5. Attaching and Detaching Roles
       - you can attach and detach roles to running EC2 instances without having to stop or
         terminate these instances. It will take immediate effect.

3.15 RDS 101

  Relational Databases
   Tables
     - data is organized in tables
     Rows: stores the data items
     Columns: The field in the database

  When to use an RDS database
    - generally used for online transaction processing (OLTP) workloads

  OLTP (OnLine Transaction Processing) and OLAP (OnLine Analytical Processing)
    OLTP
      - processes data from transactions in real time (e.g. customers orders, backing transactions,
        payments, and booking systems)
      - about data processing ad completing large numbers of small transactions in real-time
    OLAP
      - processes complex queries to analyze historical data (e.g. analyzing net profit figures from the
        past 3 yrs and sales forecasting)
      - OLAP is all about data analysis using large amounts of data, as well as complex queries that
        take a long to complete

   OLAP Example
     Net Profit Analyze: you been asked to produce a report comparing net profits for car sales in 3
       different regions
         large amount of data
             - sum of cars sold in each region
             - unit cost for each region
             - sales price of each car
         Analysis, not transactions
           - RDS is NOT suitable for analyzing large amount of data
           - use a data warehouse like Redshift, which is optimized for OLAP

  Relational Database Engines with RDS
    Microsoft SQL Server
    Oracle
    MySQL
    PostgreSQL
    MariaDB
    AWS Aurora
      - MySQL and PostgreSQL compatible

  RDS Advantages
    - up and running in minutes
    - multi-AZ (e.g. primary in one AZ and backup in another AZ)
    - Failover capability
    - Automated backups
    - A manual install of a database in your own data center could take 8 days or longer

  IAM database authentication
    - works with MySQL and PostgreSQL engines for Aurora as well as MySQL, MariaDB and RDS PostgreSQL engines for RDS
    - With this authentication method, you don't need to use a password when you connect to a DB instance.
      Instead, you use an authentication token.
    - Use authentication tokens which a unique string of characters that RDS generates on request. Authentication
      tokens are generated using AWS Signature Version 4. Each token has a lifetime of 15 minutes.
    - consider using IAM database authentication when your applications create fewer than 200 connections per second

  IAM database authentication provides the following benefits
    - Network traffic to and from the database is encrypted using SSL or TLS
    - You can use IAM to centrally manage access to your database resources, instead of managing access
      individually on each DB instance
    - For applications running on Amazon EC2, you can use profile credentials specific to your EC2 instance
      to access your database instead of a password, for greater security.

  Console Demo
    -> RDS -> create database -> select "MySQL", select MySQL version,
      template options
         - Production -> by default, multi-AZ DB cluster
         - Dev/Test
         - Free Tier (singel AZ)
       -> select EC2 instance type
       -> select EBS storage type and size
       -> include auto storage scaling
       -> enter password
       -> create

     After it creates, it provides
      endpoint and port 3306
       -> to connect, using endpoint:port along with username and password to log in and start create
          database

     After it is created, you can select DB -> Modify -> change property

  Exam Tips

    RDS Database Types:
       Microsoft SQL Server, Oracle, MySQL, PostgreSQL, MariaDB, & Amazon Aurora

    RDS is for OLTP (OnLine Transaction Processing) workloads
      - great for processing lots of small transactions like customers orders, backing transactions,
        payments, and booking systems
        - not suitable for OLAP (OnLine Analytical Processing) workloads
           - use a data warehouse like Redshift, which is optimized for OLAP

3.16 RDS Demo

   Bootstrap script
     #!/bin/bash
     yum update -y
     yum install mysql -y

   RDS Commands
      1) Check mysql has installed:
      mysql --version

      2) Connect to your database using your endpoint (specify your own endpoint):
      mysql -u acloudguru -p -h <YOUR_RDS_ENDPOINT> acloudguru

      3) Check the status of your database:
      status
      show databases;

      4) Quit the database connection:
      exit


  Lesson Objectives

    1. Launch an RDS Instance
      - using AWS console
      -> console -> RDS -> Create Database ->  "Standard create", Engine Options: MySQL,
           version: 8.0.28, templates: Free Tier;
         Settings: DB Instance Identifier: acloudguru, master username: acloudguru,
           Master Password: rdsDemoPw;
         Instance Configuration:  Burstable class: db.t3.micro;
         Storage: use defaults expect reduce max storage to 100 GiB;
         Connectivity: "Don't connect to an EC2 compute resource" (set manually), vpc: default,
           subnet: default, Public Access: No (no public IP), VPC Security Group: "create new":
           AZ: no preference, Database Port: 3306 (default)
         Database authentication: "Password authentication"
         Additional Configuration: Initial Database Name: acloudguru
         -> Create Database


       Notes:
         - with Templates: Production, Availability & Durability default is "Multi-AZ DB"
         - encryption enabled by default
         - Log exports includes options export Audit, Error, General, & Slow query logs to cloud watch logs
         - backup enabled by default with retention for 7 days (retention can be set from 1 to 35 days)
         - Maintenance: can choose window for maintenance updates to be preformed (day and time)
         - Deletion Protection option to prevent accidental DB deletion

    2. Launch an EC2 Instance
      - install a MySQL database client

      -> ec2 -> launch instance -> name: MyApplicationServer, AL2, t3.micro, create key pair: rdsDemoKp,
         Network: vpc: default, subnet: default, Auto-assign public IP: enable;
         Create SG: ec2-sg, HTTP anywhere  ;
         Advanced Details: User data:
             #!/bin/bash
             yum update -y
             # install mysql client (used to connect to mysql database)
             yum install mysql -y
         -> Create Instance

         -> use EC2 instance Connect to login
         # version mysql was installed
         $ mysql --version
           mysql  Ver 15.1 Distrib 5.5.68-MariaDB, for Linux (x86_64) using readline 5.1

         # set up rds-sg to allow incoming connections from MyApplicationServer ec2
         -> security groups -> select "rds-sg" -> inbound rules -> Add rule ->
            type: MYSQL/Aurora, Source: ec2-sg


    3. Connect to the RDS Instance
      - connect from our EC2 instance, using the MySQL database client

         -> using EC2 instance Connect login
            # Connect to your database using your endpoint (specify your own endpoint):
            # mysql -p: use password
            # $ mysql -u <DB_User_Name> -p -h <YOUR_RDS_ENDPOINT> <RDS_Database_Name>

            # Check the status of your database:

3.17 RDS Multi-AZ and Read Replicas


  Multi-AZ RDS
    - with Multi-AZ, RDS creaates an exact copy of your production database in another AZ.
    - primary in one AZ and backup/standby in another AZ
    - AWS handles the replication for you
    - when your write your production database, this write will automatically synchronize
      to the standby database
    - RDS will automatically failover the standby data in a 2nd AZ if the primary database is down


   Which RDS types can be configure as Multi-AZ:
      SQL Server, Oracle, MySQL, PostgreSQL, & MariaDB
      Note: Auroa is always Multi-AZ

  RDS Unplanned Failures and Maintenance
    - load balancer connects RDS database instance using a "connection string" (a web address for DB)
    - if RDS detects a failure, RDS will automatically point the web address to the standby DB (DNS failover)
    - RDS will automatically fail over to the standby during a failure so database operations
      can resume quickly without administrative intervention
    - RDS will also failover to the standby when performing maintenance
    - To Simulate an AZ failure iwith RDS configured for Multi-AZ, you can perform a reboot with forced failover

  RDS Multi-AZ is for disaster recovery
     - it is not for improving performance
     - you cannot connect to the standby when the primary database is active
     - you can improve performance using read replicas (covered in another section)

  RDS Multi-AZ Cluster
     - has two readable standby DB instances in different AZs (instead of one)


  Read Replicas:
  https://aws.amazon.com/rds/faqs/#Read_Replicas

  Read replicas, Multi-AZ, and multi-region deployments:
  https://aws.amazon.com/rds/features/read-replicas/#Read_replicas.2C_Multi-AZ_deployments.2C_and_multi-region_deployments

  What is a Read Replica
    - a read replica is a read-only copy of you primary database
    - great for read-heavy workloads and takes the load off you primary database
    - read-replica can be cross-AZ or cross-region
    - can create read-replicas with AWS console or CreateDBInstanceReadReplica API
    - each read replica has its own DNS (Domain Name System) endpoint
    - read-replicas can be promoted to be their own database
       - this breaks the replication

   Notes:
    - main purpose for multi-region is disaster recovery and local performance
    - main purpose for read replicas is scalability
    - main purpose for multi-AZ is deployments is availability
    - However, you may use a read replica for disaster recovery of the source DB
      instance either in the same AWS region or ain another region

  Read Replica Key Facts
    - about scaling read performance
      - primary used for scaling, not for disaster recovery
    - Requires automatic backup
      - automatic backup must be enabled in order to deploy a read replica
        Note: automatic backups are enabled by default, but can be disabled
    - multiple read replicas are supported
      - MySQL, MariaDB, & PostgreSQL allow you to create up to 15 read replicas for a given source DB instance
      - Oracle and SQL allow you to create up to 5 read replicas for a given source DB instance

  Exam Tips

       RDS Multi-AZ
         - an exact copy of your production database in another AZ
         - used for disaster recovery (DR)
         - in the event of a failure, RDS will automatically fail over to the standby instance

       RDS read-replicas
         - a read-only copy of your primary database in the same AZ, cross-AZ, or cross-region
         - used to increase or scale read performance
         - great for read-heavy workloads and takes the load off you primary database
           for read-only workloads (e.g. Business intelligence reporting jobs)

---------------------------------------------------
3.18 RDS Backups and Snapshots

  Types of RDS Backups
     Database snapshots
       - manual, ad-hoc, and user-initiated
       - provides a snapshot of the storage volume attached to the DB instance
     Automated Backups
       - enabled by default
       - RDS creates a daily backup or snapshot that run during a backup window that you define
       - transaction logs are used to replay transactions

  RDS Automated Backup
    1. Point-in-Time Recovery
      - recover your database to any point in time within a "retention period" of 1 to 35 days
    2. Full Daily Backup
      - RDS takes a full daily backup or snapshot (stored in S3) and also stores transaction logs
        throughout the day
    3. The Recovery Process
      - when you do a recovery, AWS will first choose the most recent daily backup and then apply
        transaction log relevent to the day, up to the recovery point
      - it enables a point-in-time recovery down to a second with the retention period

  RDS Automated Backups
    1. Stored in S3
      - Automated backups and stored in S3 (Note: manual backups are also stored in S3)
    2. Free Storage
      - you get free storage space equal to the size of you database. So if you have an RDS
        of 10GB, you get get 10GB worth of storage
    3. Defined backup Window
      - during the backup window, storage I/O may be suspended for a few seconds while the backup
        process initualizes, and you may experience increase latency at this time

  RDS Manual Snapshots
    1. not automated
      - DB snapshots are done manually
    2. No Retention Period
      - manual snapshots are not deleted even after you delete the original RDS instance, including
        any automated backups
    3. Backup to a known state
      - backup your DB instance in a known state as frequenntly as you wish, and then restore to that
        specific state at any time
    4. when to use
      - if you are planning a signficant update to your data, you might want to be able to restore to
        a known state, before making the changes to your data

  Restoring Your RDS Database
     - whether from automated backup or manually created snapshot:
         - the restored version of the database will always be a new RDS instance with a new DNS endpoint

  RDS Backups and Snapshots
    Encryption at Rest
      Enabling Encryption
        - enable encryption at creation time by selecting the encryption option in the console
      Integrated with KMS
        - Encryption is done using the AWS Key Management Service (KMS) service. AES-256 encryption
          Note: AES: Advanced Encryption Standard
      Includes all DB Storage
        - includes all underlying storage, automated backups, snapshots, logs, and read replicas
    Encrypting an Existing RDS Database
      - you can't enable encryption on unencrypted RDS DB instance
      - Flow to encrypt RDS DB from encrypted RDS DB:
         Unencrypted DB -> snapshot -> Encrypt snapshot -> DB restore using encrypted snapshot

  Exam Tips

    RDS Backups vs Snapshots
      RDS Automated Backup
         - Automated, enabled by default, you define the backup window
         - Point-in-Time recovery using daily backup snapshot plus transaction logs
         - retention period of 1 to 35 days
         - Can be used to recover your database to any point-in-time within your retention period
      RDS DB [Manual] Snapshot
        - User-initiated, ad-hoc
        - point-in-time snapshot only
        - no retention period; store indefinitely (till manually deleted)
        - Used to backup your DB instance to a known state and restore to that specific state at
          any time, e.g., before making a change to the database

     RDS Encryption
       1. Enable at Creation
          - includes all underlying storage, automated backups, snapshots, logs, and read replicas
          - can only enable encryption when you create the RDS DB
       2. KMS Integration
        - uses the AWS Key Management Service (KMS) service. AES-256 encryption
          Note: AES: Advanced Encryption Standard
       3. Encrypting Existing unencrypted RDS Instances
          - Take a RDS DB snapshot, create an encrypt snapshot, then create a new RDS instance
            using the encrypted snapshot

3.19 Increasing Scalability Using RDS Proxy

  Non RDS Proxy Connection:

            Client   <-------------------------------------->  MySQL
        Client Application                                     RDS DB
      Your application is pointed                         RDS DB receives information
      toward an RDS DB                                    from the application through
                                                          direct connection

  RDS Proxy Connection:

            Client   <------------->   Proxy   <------------------------>  MySQL
        Client Application            RDS Proxy                            RDS DB
      Your application is pointed   RDS Proxy pools and shares        RDS DB receives information
      toward an RDS Proxy           DB connections to assist with     from the application through
                                    application scalability and       RDS Proxy
                                    DB efficiency

  How RDS Proxy Works
    - connection pool: refers to having a pool of active connections to RDS DB so the connections
      can run more efficiently

  RDS Proxy Increase Application Availability
    Serverless and scales automatically
      - to your workload through pooling and sharing database connections
    Preserves application connections
      - during failover
    Detects failover and routes requests
      - to standby quickly
    Deployable over Multi-AZ for protection
      - from infracture failure
    Up to 66% faster failover times

  Other Use Cases
     Applications
        - with unpredictable workloads
        - that open/close database connections infrequently
        - that require availability through transient failures
        - with open but idle connections
      All these applications benefit from having a pool of active connections available to you

  Exam Tips


    RDS Proxy Connection:

            Client   <------------->   Proxy   <------------------------>  MySQL
        Client Application            RDS Proxy                            RDS DB
      Your application is pointed   RDS Proxy pools and shares        RDS DB receives information
      toward an RDS Proxy           DB connections to assist with     from the application through
                                    application scalability and       RDS Proxy
                                    DB efficiency

    RDS Proxy:
      - fits between your client application and your RDS Database
      - performs connection pooling so that maintains a pool of active connections to the backend database
      - it helps with application scalability and DB efficiency

    RDS Proxy Increases Application Availability
      Serverless and scales automatically
         - to your workload through pooling and sharing database connections
      Preserves application connections
        - during failover
      Detects failover and routes requests
        - to standby quickly
      Deployable over Multi-AZ for protection
        - from infracture failure
      Up to 66% faster failover times


      App ------->  |------|
                    | RDS  |  ----> RDS primary DB
      App ------->  | Proxy|
                    |      |  ----> RDS standby DB
      App ------->  |      |
                    |------|

3.20 Elasticache 101


  Memory is faster than disk
    - can speed up database queries by storing frequently accessed data in an in-memory cache
    - ElastiCache enables you to exactly that

  What is ElastiCache
    1. In-Memory Cache (Key Value)
      - makes it easy to deploy, operate, and scale an in-memory cache in the cloud
    2. Improves Database performance
      - it allows you to retrieve information from faster in-memory caches instead of slower
        disk-based storage
    3. Great for Read-Heavy database workloads
      - caching the results of I/O intensive database queries.
      - also for storing session data for distributed applications

  What is ElastiCache
    - a managed version of 2 open-source technologies: Memcached and Redis
    - Neither of these tools are specific to AWS, but by using ElastiCache you avoid a lot
      of the common issues you might encounter

  ElastiCache Memcached vs Redis
    Memcached
      - simple database caching solution
      - great for basic object caching
      - scales horizontally, but there is a no persistence Multi-AZ or failover
      - a good choice if you just want basic caching and you want your caching model to be
        simple as possible
      - not a database by itself
      - no backups
    Redis
      - a more sophisticated solution with enterprise features like persistence, replication,
        multi-AZ, and failover
      - supports sorting and ranking data (e.g., for gaming leaderboards) and complex data types
        like lists and hashes
      - supported as a caching solution
      - functions as a standalone non-relational/no-sql database
        - next best no-sql database after DynamoDB is Redis
      - failover and Multi-AZ support
      - supports backups

  Note:
   ElastiCache for Redis:  supports both Redis cluster and non-cluster modes and provides high
     availability via support for automatic failover by detecting primary node failures and promoting
     a replica to be primary with minimal impact. It allows for read availability for your application
     by supporting read replicas (across availability zones), to enable the reads to be served when the
     primary is busy with the increased workload.
     - All the nodes in a Redis cluster (cluster mode enabled or cluster mode disabled) must reside in the same region

   Redis Replication
     - Redis implements replication in two ways:
        1. With a single shard that contains all of the cluster's data in each node - Redis (cluster mode disabled)
        2. With data partitioned across up to 500 shards - Redis (cluster mode enabled)

   Redis Cluster mode
     - Cluster Mode comes with the primary benefit of horizontal scaling up and down of your Redis cluster, with
       almost zero impact on the performance of the cluster
     -  it allows you to scale in or out the number of shards (horizontal scaling) versus scaling up or down the node
        type (vertical scaling).
     - This means that Cluster Mode can scale to very large amounts of storage (potentially 100s of terabytes) across up
        to 90 shards, whereas a single node can only store as much data in memory as the instance type has capacity for
     - While using Redis with cluster mode enabled, there are some limitations:
        1. You cannot manually promote any of the replica nodes to primary.
        2. Multi-AZ is required.
        2. You can only change the structure of a cluster, the node type, and the number of nodes by restoring from a backup

  Typical Scenario
     1. Database under stress
       - you will be given a scenario where a particular database is under a lot of stress
     2. Find a Solution
       - you may be asked which service you should use to alleviate this
     3. Know when to use ElastiCache
        - ElastiCache is a good choice if your database is particularly read-heavy and not prone to
          frequent changing
           - if data is changing too frequently, ElastiCache will struggle to have the latest data
             available

   When ElastiCache cannot help:
     1. Heavy Write Loads
       - caching will not help alleviate heavy write loads, so you may need to scale up your database
     1. OLAP Queries
       - if your DB is feeling stress because you are performing Online Analytical Processing, then
         ElastiCache is not going to help - Thing about RedShift instead

  Exam Tips

      ElastiCache
        - an in-memory cached designed to improve read performance for read-heavy databases
        - it can front just about any database, but really excels being placed in
          front of RDS

      ElastiCache Memcached vs Redis
        Memcached
          - in-memory, key-value data store
          - one to use if object caching is your primary goal
          - a good choice if you just want basic caching and you want your caching model to be
            simple as possible
          - scales horizontally, but there is a no persistence, Multi-AZ or failover
          - if you don't need to support advanced data types or sorting
        Redis
          - in-memory, key-value data store
          - you are performing data sorting and ranking, such as gaming leaderboards
          - if you have advanced data types, such as lists and hashes
          - you need data persistence
          - if you need Multi-AZ

---------------------------------------------------
3.21 Introducing MemoryDB for Redis

  MemoryDB for Redis
    Massively Scalable
      - in-memory database (GB to > 100 TB)
     Highly Available
       - Multi-AZ
       - transactions log for recovery and durability
     Primary Database
       - the entire dataset for an application can be stored in memory (instead of a database plus a cache)
     Ultra-Fast Performance
       - supports > 160 million requests per second
       - microsecond read and single-digit millisecond write latency

  MemoryDB for Redis Suitable Uses
    Workloads
      - requiring an ulta-fast, Redis compatible primary database
    High performance
      - Applications that need an in-memory database to handle millions of requests per second
    Applications
      - data-intensive, low-latency applications that also require high scalability
    Microservices
      - highly scalable microservices architectures

  MemoryDB for Redis Example Use Case:
     - an online gaming company neeeds a way for millions of concurrent users to generate interactive
       3D digital worlds and share digital assets


  MemoryDB vs ElastiCache
     - both are in-memory data stores that help applications to access data faster than reading from disks
     ElastiCache for Redis
       Database Cache
         - in-memory database cache service
         - sits in-front of a database like RDS
       Fast but not Ultra-fast Performance
         - millisecond read latency
       Use Case
         - a website that needs to store session data for its customers
     MemoryDB for Redis
       Primary Database
         - reduce complexity by removing the need for a database plus cache
       Ultra-fast Performance
         - microsecond read and single-digit millisecond write latency
       Use Case
         - an online gaming company with millions of users sharing digital assests
       Fast but not Ultra-fast


  Exam Tips

    MemoryDB for Redis
      1. In-memory database
        - Ultra-Fast Performance
        - supports > 160 million requests per second
        - microsecond read and single-digit millisecond write latency
        - massively scalable > 100 TB
      2. Use Cases
        - high-performance, large-scale microservices applications
        - online game with millions of users sharing digital assets
      3.  MemoryDB vs ElastiCache for Redis
        - memoryDB can store your whole dataset in memory - no database required
        - ElastiCache is an in-memory cache for databases (e.g. RDS)

3.22 Demo: Systems Manager - Parameter Store - Demo

   AWS System Manager User Guide:
     https://docs.aws.amazon.com/systems-manager/latest/userguide/what-is-systems-manager.html
   AWS System Manager Parameter Store:
     https://docs.aws.amazon.com/systems-manager/latest/userguide/systems-manager-parameter-store.html
   AWS System Manager Parameter Store FAQs:
     https://aws.amazon.com/systems-manager/faq/#Parameter_Store
   AWS Secrets Manager FAQs:
     https://aws.amazon.com/secrets-manager/faqs/

  Scenario:
    - you work as a system administrator for a bank
    - you need to find a way to store parameters used by your applications. For example, license keys,
      database connections information, usernames and passwords, etc.
    - this information needs to be passed to your EC2 instances as a bootstrap script
    - you need to maintain the confidentialilty of the information and avoid hardcoding
      parameters in your code

  Parameter Store
    - a capability of AWS Systems Manager that provides secure, hierarchical storage for
      configuration data management and secrets management
    - you can store data such as passwords, database strings, Amazon Machine Images (AMI) IDs,
      and license codes as parameter values
    - you can store values as plain text of encrypted data

  Parameter Store Costs
    - it is free

  Parameter Store Limitation
    - limit to the number of parameters you can store (currently: 10,000)
    - no key rotation

 Parameter Store Demo
   -> Console -> System Manager -> Parameter Store (on left under Application Manager)
      -> Create Parameter -> Parameter Details: Name: AdobeLic, Description: Adobe Lic Key,
      Tier: Standard, Type: String, Data Type: text, Value: <random string>
      -> Create parameter

   Notes:
       Tier:
          Standard:
            - Limit of 10,000 parameters.
             - Parameter value size up to 4 KB.
             - Parameter policies are not available.
             - No additional charge.
          Advanced:
            - Can create more than 10,000 parameters.
            - Parameter value size up to 8 KB.
            - Parameter policies are available.
            - Charges apply
       Type:
         String:       Any string value.
         StringList:   Separate strings using commas.
         SecureString: Encrypt sensitive data using KMS keys from your account or another account.
                       SecureString data is encrypted and decrypted using an AWS KMS key. You can use either a
                       default KMS key provided by AWS or create and use your own AWS KMS key.


    How to pass Parameter in Parameter Store:
       - can pass parameter by name to cloudFormation
       - can be assessed by EC2, lambda, CodeDeploy, Code Pipeline, etc

  Exam Tips

    Parameter Store
       Store confidential information
         - you can store passwords, database connection strings, license code, etc.
       Plain Text or Encrypted
         - you can  store values as plain text or encrypt them
       Reference
         - you can reference your parameter using the parameter name
           (e.g. in a bootstrap script)
       Integrated with AWS Services
         - you can use Parameter Store with EC2, CloudFormation, Lambda, CodeBuild,
           CodePipeline, and CodeDeploy
