5.31 Serverless Summary - Part 2

  Lambda and VPC Access
    Enable Lambda to Access VPC resources
      - it is possible to enable lambda to access resources that are inside a private VPC
    VPC Configuration Information
      - need to provide VPC Config information to the Lambda function
      - VPC Config information: the VPC ID, private subnet IDs, and Security Group ID(s)
    Network Access
      - Lambda creates ENI (Elastic Network Interfaces) using IP Address from the private subnets.
      - need to add "AWSLambdaVPCAccessExecutionRole" policy to the Lambda Function's Role so it can
        create the ENI
      - The security group needs to allow your function to access the resources in the VPC
    CloudWatch Logs access
      - AWSLambdaBasicExecutionRole is an AWS managed policy that provides write permissions to CloudWatch Logs
      - for Lambda function to have cloudwatch logs access, its execution role needs to include
        AWSLambdaBasicExecutionRole policy


                         Private VPC
                       |------------------|
                       |  Private Subnet  |
      lambda --------> | |--------------| |
      ENI 10.0.0.5     | | EC2    RDS   | |
                       | | 10.0.0.0/24  | |
                       | |--------------| |
                       |  Security Group  |
                       |------------------|



  Step Functions
    Visualize and Orchestrate
      - Great way to visualize and orchestrate your serverless application
    Automated
      - Step functions automatically trigger and track each step.
      - The output of one step is often the input to the next step
    Logging
      - Step functions log the state of each step, so if something goes wrong you can track
        what went wrong, and where.

  Step Functions Workflows
    Standard Workflows
      Long-running (up to 1 year)
      At-most-Once
      Non-idempotent (always cause a change in state)
    Express Workflows
      Short-lived (up to 5 minutes)
      At-least-once (can run multiple times)
      Idempotent (can run a request multiple times and have no additional side effects [no  change in state])

    Synchronous Express Workflows
      - the workflow must complete before the next step begins (e.g. comfirm sucessful payment
        before sending an order)
      - Synchronous Express workflows can be used to orchestrate microservices and allow you to develop
        applications without the need to develop additional code to handle errors and retries or execute parallel tasks
    Asynchronous Express Workflows
      - other tasks are not dependent on the completion of the workflow (e.g., a messaging system)
      - Asynchronous Express workflows return confirmation that the workflow was started, but does not
        wait for the workflow to complete, and does not return the results
      - the results of the workflow can be found in CloudWatch Logs

   Step Function Task
     - A Task state ("Type": "Task") represents a single unit of work performed by a state machine.
     - A task performs work by using an activity or an AWS Lambda function, or by passing parameters to the
       API actions of other services
       - example step function task (resource field is required):
             "HelloWorld": {
               "Type": "Task",
               "Resource": "arn:aws:lambda:us-east-1:012345678901:function:HelloFunction",
               "Next": "AfterHelloWorldState",
               "Comment": "Run the HelloWorld Lambda function"
             }

   X-Ray
     Analyze and Debug
       - X-Ray helps developers analyze and debug distributed applications
       - use to debug connectivity and performance issues
       - You can use X-Ray to collect data across AWS Accounts. The X-Ray agent can assume a role to publish data into an
         account different from the one in which it is running
     Service Map
        - a visual representation of your application giving you an end-to-end view of
          the components that make up your application along with latency, HTTP status codes,
          and error codes
     X-Ray Agent and SDK
       - The X-Ray Agent/daemon must be installed on your EC2 instance
       - Use the X-Ray SDK to instrument your application to send traces to X-Ray
       - By default, the X-ray SDK uses 127.0.0.1:2000 for both trace data (UDP) and sampling (TCP).
       - Use AWS_XRAY_DAEMON_ADDRESS variable if you have configured the daemon to listen on a different port
         or if it is running on a different host (e.g. in a ECS container).


  X-Ray High-Level Configuration
    Integrated with AWS Services
      - X-Ray integrates with many AWS services like DynamoDB, Lambda, API Gateway, etc
      - The following services provide X-Ray integration: AWS Lambda, Amazon API Gateway, Elastic Load Balancing,
        AWS Elastic Beanstalk, SNS, and SQS.
        Reference: AWS X-Ray Supported AWS Services.
        https://docs.aws.amazon.com/xray/latest/devguide/xray-services.html
      - AWS X-Ray integrates with Amazon S3 to trace upstream requests to update your application's S3 buckets.
        If a service traces requests by using the X-Ray SDK, Amazon S3 can send the tracing headers to downstream
        event subscribers such as AWS Lambda, Amazon SQS, and Amazon SNS. X-Ray enables trace messages for Amazon
        S3 event notifications.
        Reference: Amazon S3 and AWS X-Ray.
        https://docs.aws.amazon.com/xray/latest/devguide/xray-services-s3.html
    Your Own Applications
      - you can also instrument your own applications to send data to X-Ray
      - For example, data aout incoming HTTP requests to your application
    Supported Platforms
      - Applications can be running on EC2, Elastic Beanstalk, environments, on-premise systems,
        and ECS
    Running X-Ray in ECS
      - For ECS, run the X-Ray daemon in it's own Docker image (container), running alongside
        your application running its Docker container(s)
    X-Ray Annotations
      - If you want to also record application specific information in the form of key-value pairs,
        use annotations to add user defined ken-value pairs to your X-Ray data - allows you to
        filter, index, and search within X-Ray, e.g. game_name=TicTacToe, game_id=2645445842
    X-Ray Sampling
      - To ensure efficient tracing and provide a representative sample of the requests that your application serves,
        the X-Ray SDK applies a sampling algorithm to determine which requests get traced.
      - Sampling rules tell the X-Ray SDK how many requests to record for a set of criteria
      - By default, the X-Ray SDK records the first request each second, and five percent of any additional requests.
      - X-Ray sampling is enabled directly from the AWS console, hence your application code does not need to change.

  X-Ray High-Level Configuration Steps
    You will need 3 things:
      1. X-Ray SDK
      2. X-Ray Daemon
      3. Instrument your application using the SDK to send the required data to X-Ray,
        e.g. data about incoming HTTP requests to your application
    - If you want to also record application specific information in the form of key-value pairs,
      use annotations to add user defined ken-value pairs to your X-Ray data - allows you to
      filter, index, and search within X-Ray, e.g. game_name=TicTacToe, game_id=2645445842


   API Gateway Caching
     Improves Performance
       - improve the performance of your APIs by caching the output of API calls to avoid calling your
         backend every time
      TTL
        - Responses are cached for the TTL period. The default TTL is 300 seconds
      Reduces the Number of API Calls made to your Application
        - API Gateway returns the cached response instead of making a new request to your application
      Invalidate Cache
        - A client of your API can invalidate an existing cache entry and reload it from the integration endpoint
          for individual requests. The client must send a request that contains the Cache-Control: max-age=0 header.
        - To grant permission for a client, attach a policy:
           { "Version": "2012-10-17", "Statement": [ { "Effect": "Allow", "Action": [ "execute-api:InvalidateCache" ],
            "Resource": [ "arn:aws:execute-api:region:account-id:api-id/stage-name/GET/resource-path-specifier" ] } ] }

   API Gateway Throttling
     Default Limits: 10K requests per second and 5K concurrent requests (you can request the defaults be increased)
     - if you exceed the limit, API Gateway will return and "429, Too Many Requests" error
     - uses throttling to prevent your API from being overwhelmed by too many requests

   API Gateway Restrict access by using CORS
     - Cross-origin resource sharing (CORS) defines a way for client web applications that are loaded in one
       domain to interact with resources in a different domain.
     - When your API's resources receive requests from a domain other than the API's own domain and you want
       to restrict servicing these requests, you must disable cross-origin resource sharing (CORS) for selected
       methods on the resource.

   Advanced API Gateway
     - you can import APIs using External definition files, e.g., OpenAPI, formerly known
       as Swagger
     - When dealing with legacy applications which use SOAP, you can configure API Gateway
       as a SOAP web service passthrough, or you can use API Gateway to convert the XML
       response to JSON

   API Gateway IAM Authorization:
     - You can enable IAM authorization for HTTP API routes.
     - When IAM authorization is enabled, clients must use Signature Version 4 (sigv4) to sign their requests
       with AWS credentials. API Gateway invokes your API route only if the client has execute-api
       permission for the route.
     - The following AWS CLI command enables IAM authorization for an HTTP API route.
          aws apigatewayv2 update-route \
              --api-id abc123 \
              --route-id abcdef \
              --authorization-type AWS_IAM

   API Gateway Resource Policy
     - JSON policy documents that you attach to an API to control whether a specified principal (typically an IAM role
       or group) can invoke the API.
     - You can use API Gateway resource policies to allow your API to be securely invoked by:
        - Users from a specified AWS account.
        - Specified source IP address ranges or CIDR blocks.
        - Specified virtual private clouds (VPCs) or VPC endpoints (in any account).
     - API Gateway resource policies are attached to resources

   API Gateway Resource Policy example:
     - The following resource policy grants API access in one AWS account to two roles in a different AWS account via
       Signature Version 4 (SigV4) protocols
     - Specifically, the developer and the administrator role for the AWS account identified by account-id-2 are granted
       the execute-api:Invoke action to execute the GET action on the pets resource (API) in your AWS account
        {
           "Version": "2012-10-17",
           "Statement": [
                {
                    "Effect": "Allow",
                    "Principal": {
                        "AWS": [
                            "arn:aws:iam::account-id-2:role/developer",
                            "arn:aws:iam::account-id-2:role/Admin"
                        ]
                    },
                    "Action": "execute-api:Invoke",
                    "Resource": [
                        "execute-api:/stage/GET/pets"
                    ]
                }
            ]
        }

   AWS Signature Version 4 protocol:
     - Authentication with AWS Signature Version 4 provides some or all of the following, depending on how you
       choose to sign your request:
          Verification of the identity of the requester
             - Authenticated requests require a signature that you create by using your access keys (access key ID, secret access key)
           In-transit data protection
             - In order to prevent tampering with a request while it is in transit, you use some of the request elements to calculate the request signature
           Protect against reuse of the signed portions of the request
             - The signed portions (using AWS Signatures) of requests are valid within 15 minutes of the timestamp in the request

5.32 Serverless Summary - Part 3


  Example of Serverless Architectures
    Event Driven and Asynchronous
      - An event or message might trigger an action, but no response is expected or required
    Build Blocks
      - Think of AWS services as building blocks that can be integrated together to create
        an application
    Event-driven architecutres are Loosely Coupled
      - SQS allows you to queue messages.
      - EventBridge help you handle events and route them to application components
    Flexibility and Scalability
      - Services and components operate and scale independently of each other

  Example Serverless Archtecture
    1. Banking Application
                                                                                      --> Lambda        SQS
                                                                                      |   Overdraft  -> Offer
     Customer     API         Lambda fcn                  DynamoDB                    |   Increase      Increase
     withdraw  -> Gateway  -> to process withdrawal  ->   alert due     -> EventBridge                  Overdraft
     request                  and update DB table         to overdraft                |
                                                                                      |--> Lambda         SNS
                                                                                           Overdraft   -> Phone notification
                                                                                           Notification   using overdraft
    2. Image Processing
                                      DynamoDB
                                  |-> Store Image
                                  |   Metadata
     S3           Lambda fcn      |
     Image    --> To process    --|
     Upload       resize image    |   S3                       CloudFront
                                  |-> ProcessedImages   <----- All procesesed images
                                                               are served by CF

    3. Processing Streaming Data from Social Media Events

      Social   in real-time     Kinesis        Lambda Fcn    places       DynamoDB
      Media   ----------------> Firehose  ---> Processes    ------------> Table
      Events   loaded time                     image data    result


  Note:
      Eventbridge (formally CloudWatch Events)
        - is a serverless event bus that was built on top of the existing AWS CloudWatch Events API
        - enables developers to integrate many of the AWS services through events.
           - For example, if there is a change in the state of an EC2 instance, a CloudWatch Event can trigger an event to
             AWS Lambda to take an action.
        - EventBridge extends this functionality beyond the AWS ecosystem, letting you brings together your own (legacy)
          applications, SaaS (Software-as-a-Service), and AWS Services.
        - It can stream real-time data from various event sources like PagerDuty, Symantec, and routes to various targets
          (AWS services) like SQS, Lambda, and others.


   Ephermal and Persistent Lambda Data Storage options

      Lambda Native Storage Options
          storage:         /tmp                Lambda Layers
          Use Case:        Temporary data      Libraries & SDKs
          Size Limit:      512MB - 10GB        50MB Zipped, 250MB unzipped*
          Dynamic Updates: Dynamic read/write  Updates require a new layer
          Shared:          Shared within       Shared across
                           Execution Env       Execution Env

      * Lambda layer size limits applies to all the files uploaded, including deployment package,
        layers, and custom runtimes, etc

      Lambda External Storage Options
          storage:         S3                  EFS
          Use Case:        Persistent Data     Persistent Data
          Size Limit:      Elastic             Elastic
          Dynamic Updates: Store and retrieve  Dynamic read/write
          Shared:          Shared across       Shared across
                           Execution Env       Execution Env


   Lambda Environment Variables
     - adust you function's behavior, without changing your code, e.g.
        key:environment,   value: development;
        key: databasename, value: mydevdb
     - An environment variable is a pair of strings that are stored in a function's version-specific
       configuration.
     - The Lambda runtime makes environment variables available to your code and sets additional environment
       variables that contain information about the function and invocation request
     - The total size of all environment variables doesn't exceed 4 KB
   Lambda Configurable Parameters
     - allow you to adjust various setting that apply to your function
     - located in the Configuration tab
     Configure Capacity
       - Memory, ephemeral storage, & concurency
     Connectivity
       - Connect to other AWS services like CloudWatch, X-Ray for monitoring, VPCs, and EFS file systems


  Other Lambda Configurable Parameters
    Monitoring and Operations tools
      - CloudWatch, CloudWatch Logs, X-Ray (performance issues)
    Concurrency
      - Concurrency is the number of requests that a Lambda function is serving at any given time.
      Reserved concurrency
        - Reserved concurrency is the maximum number of concurrent instances you want to allocate to your function
        - When a function has reserved concurrency, no other function can use that concurrency.
        - There is no charge for configuring reserved concurrency for a function
      Provisioned concurrency
        - Provisioned concurrency is the number of pre-initialized execution environments you want to allocate to your function
        - These execution environments are prepared to respond immediately to incoming function requests.
        - Configuring provisioned concurrency incurs charges to your AWS account.
      Application Auto Scaling
        https://docs.aws.amazon.com/lambda/latest/dg/provisioned-concurrency.html
        - You can use Application Auto Scaling to manage provisioned concurrency on a schedule or based on utilization
        scheduled scaling
          - If you observe predictable patterns of traffic to your function, use scheduled scaling.
        Tracking Scaling
          - If you want your function to maintain a specific utilization percentage, use a target tracking scaling policy
          - With target tracking, Application Auto Scaling creates and manages a set of CloudWatch alarms based on how you
            define your scaling policy.
    File Systems
      - EFS file systems that your function needs to connect to
      - your function must be connected to the same VPC as the file system


   Environment Variables and Parameters - Demo
     - Environment variables are user-defined key-value pairs used to adjust your function's
       behavior without changing your coded [accessed with os.getenv(<env>,<defaultValue>) ]
     - The lambda configuration tab lets you define general configuration settings, like memory
       and ephemeral storage, triggers, permissions, VPC access, tags, function URLs,
       monitoring, concurrency, and EFS file system access

   Lambda Deployment Packaging Options
      Deployment Package
        - a zip file that contains your function code and optional dependencies
        - Lambda supports two types of deployment packages: container images and .zip file archives
      Large Deployment Package
        - deployment packages over 50 MB in size must be ziped and uploaded to S3
      Lambda Layers
        Distribution Mechanism
          - a .zip file archive that can be referenced by your function containing function dependencies
            like libraries, custom runtimes, etc.
            e.g.: a zip file archive containing a specific SDK
               xray-sdk.zip
                 - nodejs/node_modules/aws-xray-sdk
        Shared
          - once created, a layer can be used by multiple functions that have the same dependencies
        Size
         - Lambda deployment package size limits*: 250 MB unzipped, 50 MB (zipped, for direct upload)
           * Lambda layer size limits applies to all the files uploaded, including deployment package,
             layers, and custom runtimes, etc
        Best Practices
          - Reduces the size of your deployment package, enable your to initialize faster
          - if you need to provide libraries or SDKs
      Lambda Container images
        - To deploy a container image to Lambda, the container image must implement the Lambda Runtime API
        - You must create the Lambda function from the same account as the container registry in Amazon ECR
        - Lambda currently supports only Linux-based container images
        - You can deploy Lambda function as container image with the maximum size of 10GB

  Lambda Performance Tuning Best Practices
    Memory and CPU
      - increasing the memory will increase CPU capacity and can reduce the duration
    Dependencies
      - importing libraries and SDKs takes time and can slow down the initialization of your function
    Only import what you need
      - only import what you need in order to avoid wasting time importing the entire SDK
      For instead of importing the entire SDK, e.g.:
         import AWS from "aws-sdk"
      if you only need DynamoDB, you import it:
        import DynamoDB from "aws-sdk/clients/dynamoDB"
    Establish Database connections
      Establishing connections within the execution environment allows them to be reused the next time
        the function is invoked, which saves time.
    Execution Environment Reuse
      - Initialize SDK clients and database connections outside of the function handler, and cache static
          assets locally in the /tmp directory.
      - Subsequent invocations processed by the same instance of your function can reuse these resources.
        This saves cost by reducing function run time
    Lambda Snapshot [Java only env]
      - Lambda SnapStart for Java can improve startup performance for latency-sensitive applications by
        up to 10x at no extra cost, typically with no changes to your function code.
      - The largest contributor to startup latency (often referred to as cold start time) is the time that
        Lambda spends initializing the function, which includes loading the function's code, starting the
        runtime, and initializing the function code.
      - With SnapStart, Lambda initializes your function when you publish a function version. Lambda takes a
        Firecracker microVM snapshot of the memory and disk state of the initialized execution environment,
        encrypts the snapshot, and caches it for low-latency access.


   Handling Lambda Event Lifecycle and Errors
     Retries
       - if a function returns and error, Lambda automatically performs 2 retries
     Dead Letter Queues
       - Save failed invocations for further processing
       - SQS and SNS are supported
       - Use SNS to fanout to multiple destinations
       - DLQ Maximum Receives is the number of times a message should be process attempts before
         for moving it to the DLQ queue (between 1 and 1000)
       - When an asynchronous invocation event exceeds the maximum age or fails all retry attempts, Lambda discards it.
         Or sends it to dead-letter queue if you have configured one

     Lambda Destinations
       - Configure one destination for sending successful invocation records and another for
         unsuccessful invocation records (e.g. SQS, SNS, Lambda, & EventBridge)

  Using API Gateway Stages for Testing Deployed Code
      Stages
      - An API Gateway stage references the lifecycle state of the API (e.g., dev, prod, v3, etc.)
      Stages Variables
      - stage variables can be used to reference a specific backend endpoint (e.g. a Lambda Function)
      - Key-value pairs that act like environment variables (note: API Gateway does not use env variables)
      - Different stages can send incoming requests to different functions (e.g, for development,
        or testing)

  API Gateway CloudWatch Logging
    - e.g. enable CloudWatch logging at the method level for the API Gateway API (i.e. calling Lambda)
    - API Gateway calls AWS STS to assume the IAM role, so STS is used by API Gateway for logging data to
      CloudWatch logs. Hence, AWS STS has to be enabled for the Region that you're using

  API Gateway Canary Deployments
    - In a canary release deployment, total API traffic is separated at random into a production release and
      a canary release with a preconfigured ratio.
    - Typically, the canary release receives a small percentage of API traffic and the production release takes up the rest.
    - The updated API features are only visible to API traffic through the canary.
    - You can adjust the canary traffic percentage to optimize test coverage or performance

  API Gateway Mock Endpoints Overview
    - mock endpoints simulate the responses and behaviors that you would expect from an API
    - create, test, and debug without requiring the real API to be available
    - common use case: a development team has finished create frontend components and is ready
      to test, but the backend components are not ready
    - Use an API Gateway mock integration endpoint to simulate teh backend response


  API Gateway Response and Request Transformations
     Transform
       - API Gateway can transform HTTP requests and responses using parameter mappings
     Request Transformation
       - can change the header, query string (query, data, payload), or request path (URL to
         route request to appropriate endpoint) of an API request
     Response Transformation
       - can change the header or status code of an API response

  API Mapping Templates [script used to transform requests and responses]
    - You can use mapping templates to transform your data.
    - A mapping template is a script expressed in Velocity Template Language (VTL) and applied to the payload using JSONPath
    - an API's method request or response can take a payload in a different format from the integration request or response

  API Gateway Lambda authorizers
    - A Lambda authorizer (formerly known as a custom authorizer) is an API Gateway feature that uses a
      Lambda function to control access to your API.
    - A Lambda authorizer uses bearer token authentication strategies, such as OAuth or SAML.
    - Before creating an API Gateway Lambda authorizer, you must first create the AWS Lambda function that
      implements the logic to authorize and, if necessary, to authenticate the caller

  API Gateway supported authentication and authorization mechanisms:
    - resource policies
    - Standard AWS IAM Roles and policies
    - IAM Tags - togehther with IAM policies to control access
    - endpoint policies for interfaces VPC endpoints
    - Lambda authorizer
    - Cognito User Pools

  API Gateway Usage Plans
    - A usage plan specifies who can access one or more deployed API stages and methods - and also how much
      and how fast they can access them.
    - The plan uses API keys to identify API clients and meters access to the associated API stages for each key.

---------------------------------------------------
5.33 AWS Certified Developer - Associate - Serverless


Question 8
... What are the three different ways you can deploy your code to Lambda?

Choose 3 - Correct Choices
- Copy and paste your code in to the integrated development environment (IDE) inside Lambda.
- Zip your code into a zip file and upload it via the Lambda console.
- Write a CloudFormation template that will deploy your environment including your code.

Correct. The code editor in the AWS Lambda console enables you to write, test, and view the execution results of your
Lambda function code.

Correct. A deployment package is a ZIP archive that contains your function code and dependencies. You can upload the package
directly to Lambda, or you can use an Amazon S3 bucket, and then upload it to Lambda. If the deployment package is larger than
50 MB, you must use Amazon S3.

Correct. The CloudFormation AWS::Lambda::Function resource creates a Lambda function. To create a function, you need a deployment package and an execution role.

Question 11
Which of the following services does X-Ray integrate with?

Choose 4
 - Amazon Elastic File System (EFS)
 - Amazon CloudFront
 - Amazon S3                  -> Correct Answer
 - AWS Lambda                 -> Correct Answer
 - Elastic Load Balancer      -> Correct Answer
 - API Gateway                 -> Correct Answer
 - Amazon Elastic Block Store (EBS)

Correct Answer

AWS X-Ray integrates with Amazon S3 to trace upstream requests to update your application's S3 buckets. If a service traces
requests by using the X-Ray SDK, Amazon S3 can send the tracing headers to downstream event subscribers such as AWS Lambda,
Amazon SQS, and Amazon SNS. X-Ray enables trace messages for Amazon S3 event notifications. Reference: Amazon S3 and AWS X-Ray.

The following services provide X-Ray integration: AWS Lambda, Amazon API Gateway, Elastic Load Balancing, AWS Elastic Beanstalk,
Amazon Simple Notification Service, and Amazon Simple Queue Service. Reference: AWS X-Ray Supported AWS Services.


Question 20

You are a developer for a busy real estate company, and you want to enable other real estate agents to have the ability to show
properties on your books, but skinned so that it looks like their own website. You decide the most efficient way to do this
is to expose your API to the public using API Gateway. The project works well, but one of your competitors starts abusing this
by sending your API tens of thousands of requests per second. This generates an HTTP 429 error. Each agent connects to your API
using individual API keys. What actions can you take to stop this behavior?

Choose 2
 - Deploy multiple API Gateways and give the agent access to another API Gateway
 - Throttle the agent's API access using the individual API Keys                                   <- Correct Answer
 - Use AWS Shield Advanced API protection to block the requests
 - Place an AWS Web Application Firewall (AWS WAF) in front of API Gateway and filter the requests  <- Correct Answer

Correct Answer

To prevent your API from being overwhelmed by too many requests, Amazon API Gateway throttles requests to your API using
the token bucket algorithm, where a token counts for a request. You can enable usage plans to restrict client request
submissions to within specified request rates and quotas. This restricts the overall request submissions so that they
don't go significantly past the account-level throttling limits in a Region. Amazon API Gateway provides Per-client
throttling limits that are applied to clients that use API keys associated with your usage policy as client identifier.

AWS WAF helps protect your web applications or APIs against common web exploits that could impact availability,
compromise security, or consume excessive resources.


Question 18

Which of the following features are used when modifying an HTTP request or response that is being processed by API Gateway,
for instance, to change an HTTP header or status code?

Choose 2
 - Request and response transformations     <- correct answer
 - Stage variables
 - Integrations
 - Parameter mapping                         <- correct answer

Stage variables are used to influence the behavior of API Gateway. For instance, they can be used to determine which stage
an incoming API request should be routed to (e.g., prod, dev, or test). They are not used for modifying an HTTP request or
response.

Correct Answer

A request transformation is used to modify an HTTP request that is being processed by API Gateway, for instance, to change an
HTTP header. A response transformation is used to modify an HTTP response that is being processed by API Gateway, for instance,
to change an HTTP status code.

Parameter mapping is used to configure API Gateway request and response transformations, for instance, to change an
HTTP header or status code.

Chapter 6 DynamoDB

6.1 Introduction to DynamoDB

  DynamoDB FAQ:
  https://aws.amazon.com/dynamodb/faqs/

  New DynamoDB Standard-Infrequent Access table class
  https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2021/12/amazon-dynamodb-standard-infrequent-access-table-class/

  DynamoDB Transactions: How it works
  https://docs.aws.amazon.com/amazondynamodb/latest/developerguide/transaction-apis.html

  Service, account, and table quotas in Amazon DynamoDB
  https://docs.aws.amazon.com/amazondynamodb/latest/developerguide/ServiceQuotas.html

  What is DynamoDB
    Fast and Flexible NoSQL database
      - DynamoDB is a fast and flexible NoSQL database service for all applications that need
        consistent, single-digit millisec latency at any scale
    Full Managed
       - a fully managed database and support both document and key-value data models
       - Supported document formats are JSON, HTML, and XML
    Use Cases
      - flexible data model and reliable performance make it a great fit for mobile, web,
        gaming, ad-tech, IoT, and many other applications

  DynamoDB - Serverless
      - integrates well with Lambda
      - can be configured to automatically scale
      - A popular choice for developers and architects who are designing serverless applications

  DynamoDB Features
    Performance
      - stored on SSD storage
    Resilience
      - spread across 3 geographically distinct data centers
    Consistency - 2 choices:
      - eventually consistent reads (default)
      - optionally, strongly consistent reads

  DynamoDB Consistency Models
    Eventually consistent reads
      - consistency across all copies of the reached within a second
      - repeating a a read after a short time should return the updated data
      - Best read peformance
    Strongly consistent reads
      - returns a result that reflects all writes that receive a successful response
        prior to the read
      - Writes are reflected across all 3 locations at once
      - Best for read consistency
    ACID Transactions
      - DynamoDB Transactions provide the ability to perform ACID Transactions (Atomic,
        Consistent, Isolated, Durable)


  ACID (Atomic Consistent Isolated Durable) Diagram
    Atomic
      - all changes to the data must be performed successfully or not at all
    Consistent
      - data must be in a consistent data before and after the transaction
    Isolated
      - no other process can change the data while the transaction is running
    Durable
      - the changes made by a transction must persist

  ACID with DynamoDB
   - DynamoDB transactions provide developers atomicity, consistency, isolation,
     and durability (ACID) across 1 or more tables within a single AWS account and region
   - you can use transactions when building applications that require coordinated inserts,
     deletes, or updates to multiple items as part of a single logical business operation

    All or Nothing
    ACID basically means all or nothing
    - All: transaction succeeds across 1 or more tables
    - Nothing: whole transaction fails

  DynamoDB Primary Keys
    What is a Primary Key?
      - DynamoDB stores and retrieves data based on a primary key
    Primary Key 2 Types:
      - Partition Key
      - Composite Key (partition key + sort key)

  DynamoDB Primary Keys
    Partition Key
      - based on a unique attribute
      - e.g. customer_ID, product_ID, email address, vehicle registration number
      - value of the partition key is input to an internal hast function which determines the partition or
        physical location on which the data is stored
      - If you are using the partition key as your primary key, then no 2 items can have same partition key
    Composite Key
      Partition Key + Sort Key
      - used when partition key is not unique in your table
      - example - Formum Posts where users post multiple message so user id alone is not unique
           Combination of:
              - User_ID
              - Sort key (post timestamp)
      A Unique Combination
        - items in the table may have the same partition key, but they must have a different sort key
      Storage
        - All items with the same partition key are stored together and then sorted according to the sort key value

  DynamoDB example:

      {
        "StudentID": "1975",        <---- Partition Key because it is unique
        "FirstName": "Allan",
        "Surname": "Brown"
        "Phone": "555-2323"
      }
      {
        "StudentID": "1976",         <---- Partition Key but Not unique because student can be enrolled in multiple courses
        "FirstName": "Riad",
        "Surname: "Ramonov"
        "CourseName": "AWS_Developer_Associate"    <--- Sort Key
        "Address" " {
              "Number" : "5"
              "Street" : "River Road"
              }
      }



  Exam Tips

    DynamoDB
      What is DynamoDB?
       - a low latency NoSQL database
      Data Models
        - supports both document and key-value data models
        - Supported document formats are JSON, HTML, and XML
      Consistency Models
        Eventually consistent reads
          - consistency across all copies of the reached within a second
          - repeating a a read after a short time should return the updated data
          - Best read peformance
        Strongly consistent reads
          - returns a result that reflects all writes that receive a successful response
            prior to the read
          - Writes are reflected across all 3 locations at once
          - Best for read consistency
        ACID Transactions
          - DynamoDB Transactions provide the ability to perform ACID Transactions (Atomic,
            Consistent, Isolated, Durable)
          - ACID basically means all or nothing
            - All: transaction succeeds across 1 or more tables
            - Nothing: whole transaction fails

      DynamoDB Features
        - consists of tables, items (rows or records in database), and attributes (columns in the table)
        2 Types of Primary Keys
           Partition Key
             - based on a unique attribute
             - e.g. customer_ID, product_ID, email address, vehicle registration number
           Composite Key (partition key + sort key)
              - used when partition key is not unique in your table
              - example - Formum Posts where uses post multiple message so user id alone is not unique
                   Combination of:
                      - User_ID
                      - Sort key (post timestamp)

6.3 DynamoDB Access Control

  DynamoDB Using IAM policy conditions for fine-grained access control
  https://docs.aws.amazon.com/amazondynamodb/latest/developerguide/specifying-conditions.html

  Controlling Access to DynamoDB
    IAM
      - Authentication and access control is managed using AWS IAM
    IAM Permissions
      - you can create IAM users within your AWS account with specific permissions
        to access and create DynamoDB tables
    IAM Roles
      - you can create IAM roles, enabling temporary access to DynamoDB

  Restricting User Access
    - you can also use a special IAM condition to restrict user access to only their
      own records (instead of the entire table)
    Example: Mobile Gaming Application with Millions of Players
      - user need to access their own top score for each game that they are playing.
      - Access must be restricted to ensure they cannot view anyone else's data
    - this can be done by adding a condition to an IAM Policy to allow access to only items
      where the partition key value matches their user_id

  DynamoDB example policy

       {

         "Version": "2012-10-17",
         "Statement": [
           {
            "Sid" : "AllowAccessToOnlyItemsMatchingUserId",        <---- Statement Identifier
            "Effect": "Allow",
            "Action": [
                "dynamodb:GetItem",
                "dynamodb:PutItem",                                 <---- Defines the action that
                "dynamodb:UpdateItem"                                     the policy allows
            ],
            "Resource": ["arn:aws:dynamodb:us-east-1:*:*:table/HighScores"],
            "Condition": {
                "ForAllValues:StringEquals": {                             Allows user to access only
                    "dynamodb:LeadingKeys": [                        <---- the items where the partition
                        "${www.mygame.com:user_id-name-3}"                 key value matches their user ID
                    ],
                    "dynamodb:Attributes": [
                       "UserId",
                       "GameTitle",                                   <---- Defines the attributes that
                       "TopScore",                                          that the policy applies to
                       "TopScoreDateTime"
                     ]
                  },
                  "StringEqualsIfExists": {
                  "dynamodb:Select": "SPECIFIC_ATTRIBUTES"
                  }
                  }
           }
           ]
           }

  Exam Tips

     DynamoDB Access Control
       - Fine-Grained Access Control with IAM
       IAM Condition parameter
         - dynambodb:LeadingKeys allows user to access only the items where the partition
           key value matches their User_ID

---------------------------------------------------
6.4 Indexes Deepdive - Understanding DynamoDB Secondary Indexes

  What is a Secondary Index
    Flexible Querying
      - Query based on an attribute that is not the primary key
      - DynamoDB allows your to run a query on non-primary key attributes using
        "global secondary indexes" and "local secondary indexes"
      - A secondary index allows you to perform fast queries on specific columns in a table
      - you select the columns that your want included in the index and run your searches on
        the index, rather that on the entire dataset

  Local Secondary Index
    Primary key
      - Has same partition key as your original table but a different sort key
      - only can be created when you are creating your table
    A Different View
      - Gives you a different view of your data, organized according to the alternative sort key
    Faster Queries
      - Any queries based on this sort key are much faster using the index than the main table
    Example
       Partition key: user ID
       sort key: account creation date (different original sort key than used with the primary key)
    Add at Creation Time
      - local secondary index key Can only be specified when you ar creating your table. You cannot
        add, remove, or modify it later

  Global Secondary Index
    A completely different Primary key
      - has a different partition key and sort key
    View Your Data Differently
      - Gives a completely different view of the data
    Speeds up Queries
      - speeds up any query relating to this alternative partition and sort key
    Example
       Partition key: email address
       Sort key: last log in date
    Flexible
      - you can create it when you create your table, or add it later



  Exam Tips

    DynamoDb Secondary Indexes
     - index enable faster queries on specific data columns
     - give you a different view of your data based on the alternative paritition / sort key
     Local Secondary Index
       - same partition key and different sort key to your table
       - must be created when you create your table
     Global Secondary Index
       - different partition key and different sort to your table
       - can be created at any time

---------------------------------------------------
6.5 AWS DynamoDB in the Console - Creating Tables, Items, and Indexes


About this lab
Welcome to this live AWS environment for learning the fundamentals of using DynamoDB in the AWS console. This lab provides
you with essential exposure to database concepts such as partition and sort keys, how to add, delete, and update items,
as well as attributes inside of a DynamoDB table. Indexes give access to alternate query patterns and can speed up queries.
Secondary keys allow efficient access to data with attributes other than the primary key. You can create one or more secondary
indexes on a table. Query or Scan requests can be done against those indexes. In the lab, you will add local and global secondary
indexes, and use the local secondary index to query for items in your table.

Learning objectives
 - Create a DynamoDB Table
 - Add Items to Your Table
 - Add a Global Secondary Index to your Table

Create a DynamoDB Table

    Navigate to the DynamoDB console.
    Click Tables in the left-hand column of the DynamoDB console.
    Click Create table, and set the following values in the Table details section:
        Table name: Enter MusicNotes.
        Partition key: Enter Artist, and select String.
        Sort Key: Enter SongTitle, and select String.
    In the Settings section, select Customize settings.
    In the Read/Write capacity settings section, set the following values:
        Capacity mode: Provisioned
        Read Capacity Auto Scaling: Off
        Read Provisioned capacity units: 1*
        Write Capacity Auto Scaling: Off
        Write Provisioned capacity units: 1
    Click on Create local index in the Secondary indexes section.
    In the New local secondary index dialog, set the following values:
        Sort key: Rating, Number
        Index name: Artist-Rating-index
        Attribute projections: All
    Click Create index.
    Click Create table and give it a few minutes for table creation to complete.

Creating Items

    Click on Explore items in the left-hand column of the DynamoDB console.
    Select the MusicNotes table.
    Click on Create item.
    Set the following values:
        Artist: Fernando
        SongTitle: Sings
    Click Add new attribute > Number.
    In the new string after SongTitle, set Rating to 6.
    Click Create item.
    Select the checkbox next to the item we just created.
    Select Actions > Edit item.
    Click on Add new attribute under the Rating attribute and select String from the drop down menu.
    In the new attribute line after Rating set the following values:
        Attribute name: Genre
        Value: Rap
    Click Save changes.
    Create another new item of your own so we can search for it later with the Genre set to Rock. Be sure to append a new string and provide both the Rating and the Genre.

Creating a Global Secondary Index

    Click Tables in the left-hand column of the DynamoDB console.
    Select the MusicNotes table and click on the Indexes tab.
    Click Create index in the Global secondary indexes section.
    In the Create global secondary index dialog, set the following values:
        Partition key: Genre, String
        Sort key: Rating, Number
        Index name: Genre-Rating-index
    Click Create index.
    It can take several minutes for the index to be created. Once the index is created, the Status will change to Active, although we may have to reload the page to see the status change.

Query a DynamoDB Table

    Click on Explore items in the left-hand column of the DynamoDB console.
    Select the MusicNotes table.
    Click on the MusicNotes table name at the top of the page and the Scan and Query section should expand out.
    Select Query.
    Make sure the Table or index value is set to MusicNotes.
    For the Partition key, enter Fernando.
    For the Sort key, set the following:
        Begins with: Sings
    Click Run.
        Only songs by the artist Fernando and song titles beginning with Sings will be shown in the results.
    Change the Table or index value to Artist-Rating-index.
    For the Partition key, enter Fernando.
    For the Sort key, set the following:
        Equal to: 6
    Click Run.
        Only songs by artist Fernando with a rating of 6 will be shown in the results list.
    Change the Table or index value to Genre-Rating-index.
    For the Partition key, enter Rock.
    For the Sort key, setBetween to 1 and 10.
    Click Run. Only songs with the genre Rock will be shown in the results list.

6.6 Scan vs Query API Call - Demo

  Amazon DynamoDB > API Actions
  https://docs.aws.amazon.com/amazondynamodb/latest/APIReference/API_Operations.html

  DynamoDB - Best practices for querying and scanning data
  https://docs.aws.amazon.com/amazondynamodb/latest/developerguide/bp-query-scan.html


  What is DynamoDB Query?
    - finds items in a table based on the primary key attribute and a distint value to search for
    - For example, selecting an item where the user ID is equal to 212 will select all attributes for
      that item (e.g. first name, surname, email address)

    - by default, a query returns all the attributes for items you select, but you can use the
      "ProjectionExpression" parameter if you want to only return the specific attributes you want
      (e.g. if you only want to see the email address rather than all the attributes)

  Refine your Query
    - use an optional sort key name and value to refine the results
    - for example, if your sort key is a timestamp, you can refine the query to only select items with
      a timestampe of the last 7 days

  DynamoDB Query
    Sort Key
      - Results are always sorted by the sort key
    Numeric Order
      - in ascending numeric order by default
    ASCII
      - ASCII character code values
      - sorted in ascending order by default
    Reversed the Order
      - you can reverse the order by setting "ScanIndexForward" parameter to "false"
      - "ScanIndexForward" parameter only applies to Queries (even though it has "Scan" in its name)
    Eventually Consistency
      - by default, queries are eventually consistent
    Strongly Consistency
      - you need to explicity set the query to be strongly consistent


  What is a DynamoDB Scan
    - a scan examines every item in the table
    - by default, it returns all data attributes
    - use the ProjectExpression" (--projection-expression) parameter to refine the scan to only return
      the attributes you want (e.g. if you only want to see the email address rather than all the attributes)

  Demo
     -> in cloudshell

       # create dynamodb ProductCatolog table with AttributeName: Id, AttributeType: Number:
       #   PrimaryKey: Id, keyType=HASH  (Note: HASH means primary key)
       $ aws dynamodb create-table --table-name ProductCatalog --attribute-definitions \
         AttributeName=Id,AttributeType=N --key-schema \
         AttributeName=Id,KeyType=HASH \
         --provisioned-throughput ReadCapacityUnits=5,WriteCapacityUnits=5

       # download table items file from github
       $ curl -O https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ACloudGuru-Resources/course-aws-certified-developer-associate/main/Create_A_DynamoDB_Table_Demo/items.json

        # dynamodb table with items in "items.json" file
        $ aws dynamodb batch-write-item --request-items file://items.json

     -> console -> DynamoDB -> Tables (left tab) -> ProductCatalog Table -> Explore Table Items
        -> Scan or query items -> Query -> Id (partition key) 201 -> Run
           # returns product id 201
        -> Scan or query items -> Scan -> Run
           # returns all items
        -> Scan or query items -> Scan  Filter: Price  Type: Number  Condition: Greater Than  Value: 100
           # returns items with price > $100

  Query or Scan
    Query is more efficient than a Scan
      - a scan dumps the entire table and filters out the values to provide the desired result,
        more the unwanted data
    Scan adds an Extra Step
      - Adds an extra step of removing the data you don't want. As the table grows, the scan
        operations takes longer
    Provisioned Throughput
      - a scan operation on a large table can use up the provisioned throughput for a large table
        in a just a single operation



  How to Improve your Scan Performance
    Sequential by default
      - a scan operation processes data sequentially returning 1MB increments before moving
        on to retrieving the next 1MB of data
      - scans one partition at a time
    Parallel is Possible
      - you can configure DynamoDB to use parallel scans instead by logically dividing a table
        or index into segments and scanning each segment in parallel
    Beware
      - It is best to avoid parallel scans if your table or index is already incurring heavy read
        or write activity from other applications

  How to Improve your Scan Performance
    - Isolate scan operations to specific tables and segragate them from your mission-critical traffic
       - even if that means writing data to 2 different tables

  Improving Query and Scan Performance
    Improving Performance
      - set a smaller page size (e.g. set the page size to return 40  items)
      - running a larger number of smaller operations will allow other requests to succeed
        without throttling
    Avoid Scans!
      - Avoid using scan operations if you can
      - Design tables in a way that you can use the "Query", "Get", or "BatchGetItem" APIs

  Exam Tips

     DynamoDB Scan vs Query
       - a scan operation examines every item in the table and by default returns all data attributes
       - For queries and scans, use the "ProjectExpression" parameter to refine the results
         - also can use filter to refine your results
       DynamoDB Query
          - finds items in a table
          - using only the primary key attribute, you provide the primary key name and a distinct
            value to search for
          Query  Results
            Sort Key
              - sorted by in ascending order by the sort key, if there is one
            Reverse Order
              - set "ScanIndexForward" parameter to "false" to reverse the order

       Increase performance of query or scan
         - reduce the impact of a query or scan by setting a smaller page size
         - isolate scan operations to specific tables
         - try parallel scans rather than the default sequential scans
         - A query operation is generally more efficient than a scan
         - design tables in a way that you can use "Query", "Get" or "BatchGetItem" APIs
         DynamoDB Scan

6.7 Using DynamoDB API Calls

  DynamoDB Command Line Reference
  https://awscli.amazonaws.com/v2/documentation/api/latest/reference/dynamodb/index.html

  DynamoDB API Calls
   - use API calls to programmatically interact with our DynamoDB table (e.g. query, delete, or add items).
   - When we use teh aWS CLI to run the "get-item" command, we are interacting with the DynamoDB "GetItem" API

  DynamoDB Most Commonly Used Commands

   AWS CLI              Coresponding
   Command              API Call                  Usage
   --------------       -----------        ------------------------------------------------------------------
   create-table         CreateTable        Create a new table
   put-item             PutItem            Adds a new item into a table or replaces an old item with a new one
   get-item             GetItem            Returns a set of attributes for an item with the given primary key
   batch-get-item       BatchGetItem       Returns the attributes of one or more items from one or more tables.
                                            You identify requested items by primary key
   transact-get-items   TransactGetItem    Synchronous read operation that groups up to 100 Get actions together.
                                             Performed atomically so that either all of them succeed or all of them fail
   update-item          UpdateItem         Allows you to edit the attributes of an existing item (e.g., add or delete attributes)
                                             or adds a new item to the table if it does not already exist
   batch-write-item     BatchWriteItem     Puts or deletes multiple items in one or more tables.
   transact-write-items TransactWriteItems a synchronous and idempotent write operation that groups up to 100 write actions in a
                                            single all-or-nothing operation.
   update-table         UpdateTable        Used to modify a table (e.g. modify the provisioned throughput settings of the table)
   list-tables          ListTables         Returns a list of tables in your account
   describe-table       DescribeTable      Returns information about the table (e.g. current status, creation data,
                                            primary key, and indexes)
   scan                 Scan               Reads every item in a table and returns all items and attributes
                                            Use a FilterExpression to return fewer items
   query                Query              Queries the table based on a partition key value that you provide
   delete-item          DeleteItem         Allows you to delete an item based on its primary key
   delete-table         DeleteTable        Deletes a table including all of its items

  Do I need to know all of these DynamoDB commands:
     - you should understand what they are used for at a high-level, understand the difference between them,
       and be aware that the user must have the correct IAM permissions

  Example
    - a user needs to add a new item into a table and replace and old item with a new one

         user -> put-item CLI command -> DynamoDB table
                 PutItem API call
                 Need IAM to call "put-item"

  The official AWS CLI Command Reference
     https://awscli.amazonaws.com/v2/documentation/api/latest/reference/dynamodb/index.html

  Exam Tips

     DynamoDB CLI Commands
       - know how to use each of the common commands listed in this lesson
       - Understand that the CLI commands are making calls to a DynamoDB API
       - the correct IAM permissions are required to make the API call
          e.g.  User --> get-item
                         GetItem   -> requires IAM permissions
