9.29 Developer Theory Summary - Part 1

  CodeCommit
    Source and version control
     - source control service enabling teams to collaborate on cdel, html pages, scripts, images,
      binaries

  CodeBuild
    - AWS CodeBuild is a fully managed build service in the cloud.
    - CodeBuild compiles your source code, runs unit tests, and produces artifacts that are ready to deploy.
    - CodeBuild eliminates the need to provision, manage, and scale your own build servers.
    - It provides prepackaged build environments for popular programming languages and build tools such as
      Apache Maven, Gradle, and more.
    - You can also customize build environments in CodeBuild to use your own build tools.
    - CodeBuild scales automatically to meet peak build requests
    Automated Build
      - compiles source code, runs tests, and produces packages that are ready to deploy

  CodeDeploy
    Automated Deployment
      - Automates code deployments to EC2 instances, Lambda, and on-premise systems

  CodePipeline
    Manages the Workflow
      - end-to-end solution, allowing your to build, test, and deploy your application
        every time there is a code change

  CodeStar
   - a one-stop dashboard for all the CI/CD needs of one of your projects.
   - If you don't need heavy control of the individual configuration of each component in your CI/CD,
     but need to be able to get a holistic view of your projects
    that allow you to easily manage access and add owners, contributors, and viewers to your projects.
   - Each CodeStar project comes with a unified project dashboard and integration with Atlassian JIRA software,
     a third-party issue tracking and project management tool.

  CI/CD
    Continuous Integration
      - integrating or merging the code changes frequently - at least once per day
      - Think CodeCommit (a privately managed Git repository)
    Continuous Delivery
      - automating the build, test, and deployment functions
      - Think CodeBuild and CodeDeploy
    Continuous Deployment
      - Fully automated release process, code is deployed into Staging or Production as soon as
        it has successfully passed through the release pipeline
      - Think CodePipeline

  AWS Whitepaper
    - Practicing Continuous Integration & Continuous Deployment on AWS
       - explains the features and benefits of using continuous integration, continuous delivery (CI/CD)
         and AWS tooling in your software development enviroment

  CI/CD Whitepaper: downloaded to:
  what_is_ci_cd_lesson_9_1/practicing-continuous-integration-continuous-delivery.pdf
OR
  https://docs.aws.amazon.com/whitepapers/latest/practicing-continuous-integration-continuous-delivery/welcome.html   -> click on "PDF"


  CodeCommit
    Centralized Code Repository
      - A place to store source code, binaries, libraries, images, HTML files, etc.
      - a fully managed private Git repository
    Enables Collaroration
      - manages updates from multiple users
    Version Control
      - tracks and managed code changes
      - maintains version historyo
    Secure connections to CodeCommit repository
      - AWS allows you to use either the HTTPS or the SSH protocol to connect to CodeCommit repositories.
    Encrypted
      - Data in CodeCommit repositories is encrypted in transit and at rest.
      - When data is pushed into an CodeCommit repository (for example, by calling git push), CodeCommit encrypts
        the received data as it is stored in the repository.

  CodeCommit Credentials
     IAM supports CodeCommit with three types of credentials:
        Git credentials (generated from IAM):
          - These are IAM generated user name and password pair you can use to communicate with CodeCommit repositories over HTTPS
        SSH keys:
          - a locally generated public-private key pair that you can associate with your IAM user to communicate
            with CodeCommit repositories over SSH.
        AWS access keys:
           - you can use with the credential helper included with the AWS CLI to communicate with CodeCommit repositories over HTTPS.


  CodeBuild Run Localling with CodeBuild Agent
    - With the Local Build support for AWS CodeBuild, you just specify the location of your source code, choose
      your build settings, and CodeBuild runs build scripts for compiling, testing, and packaging your code.
    - You can use the AWS CodeBuild agent to test and debug builds on a local machine.
    - By building an application on a local machine you can:
        - Test the integrity and contents of a buildspec file locally.
        - Test and build an application locally before committing.
        - Identify and fix errors quickly from your local development environment.

  CodeBuild CloudWatch Metrics
    - CodeBuild monitors functions on your behalf and reports metrics through Amazon CloudWatch.
    - CloudWatch metrics include the number of total builds, failed builds, successful builds, and the duration of builds.
    - You can monitor your builds at two levels: Project level, AWS account level.
    - You can export log data from your log groups to an Amazon S3 bucket and use this data in custom processing
      and analysis, or to load onto other systems.

  CodeBuild Buildspec
    - A buildspec is a collection of build commands and related settings, in YAML format, that CodeBuild uses to run a build.
    - You can include a buildspec as part of the source code or you can define a buildspec when you create a build project
    - by default, the buildspec file must be named "buildspec.yml" and placed in the root of your source directory
    - buildspec syntax:
         version: 0.2

         run-as: Linux-user-name                <---- (optional) run as Linux user

         env:                                   <----  (optional) information for one or more custom environment variables
           shell: shell-tag
           variables:
             key: "value"
             key: "value"
           parameter-store:                     <----- To retrieve env variables Parameter Store, you must add the ssm:GetParameters
             key: "value"                               action to your CodeBuild service role (note: ssm: Simple System Manager)
             key: "value"
           exported-variables:
             - variable
             - variable
           secrets-manager:                      <----- Required if you want to retrieve custom env variables stored in ecrets Manager
             key: secret-id:json-key:version-stage:version-id
           git-credential-helper: no | yes

         proxy:                                   <---- (optional) Used to represent settings if you run your build in an explicit proxy server
           upload-artifacts: no | yes
           logs: no | yes

         batch:                                   <----
           fast-fail: false | true
           # build-list:
           # build-matrix:
           # build-graph:

         phases:                                   <---- Required sequence. Represents the commands CodeBuild runs during each phase of the build
           install:
             run-as: Linux-user-name
             on-failure: ABORT | CONTINUE
             runtime-versions:
               runtime: version
               runtime: version
             commands:
               - command
               - command
             finally:
               - command
               - command
             # steps:
           pre_build:
             run-as: Linux-user-name
             on-failure: ABORT | CONTINUE
             commands:
               - command
               - command
             finally:
               - command
               - command
             # steps:
           build:
             run-as: Linux-user-name
             on-failure: ABORT | CONTINUE
             commands:
               - command
               - command
             finally:
               - command
               - command
             # steps:
           post_build:
             run-as: Linux-user-name
             on-failure: ABORT | CONTINUE
             commands:
               - command
               - command
             finally:
               - command
               - command
             # steps:
         reports:                                   <---- (Optional) Specifies the report group that the reports are sent to
           report-group-name-or-arn:
             files:
               - location
               - location
             base-directory: location
             discard-paths: no | yes
             file-format: report-format
         artifacts:                                   <----
           files:
             - location
             - location
           name: artifact-name
           discard-paths: no | yes
           base-directory: location
           exclude-paths: excluded paths
           enable-symlinks: no | yes
           s3-prefix: prefix
           secondary-artifacts:
             artifactIdentifier:
               files:
                 - location
                 - location
               name: secondary-artifact-name
               discard-paths: no | yes
               base-directory: location
             artifactIdentifier:
               files:
                 - location
                 - location
               discard-paths: no | yes
               base-directory: location
         cache:                                   <----
           paths:
             - path
             - path



  CodeDeploy
    CodeDeploy Deployment Approaches
      In-Place Deployment
        - The application is stopped on each instance and then the new release is installed
        - Also known as a Rolling Update
        - Capacity is reduced during the deployment
        - Lambda is not supported
        - Rolling back involves a re-deploy a previously revision
        - Great when deploying the first time
      Blue/Green deployment
        - new instances are provisioned and the new release is installed on the new instances
        - Blue represents the active deployment, green is the new release
        - No capacity reduction
        - Green instances can be created ahead of time
        - Easy to switch between old and new
        - you pay for 2 environments until you terminate the old [blue] servers
        - safest option
    Compute platform
       - A compute platform is a platform on which CodeDeploy deploys an application
       There are three compute platforms
         - EC2/On-Premise
         - AWS Lambda
         - ECS (ECS EC2 and ECS Fargate)
     CodeDeploy to EC2
        - attach IAM instance role to the EC2 with codecommit:GitPull permssions for codecommit repository ARN
          (e.g. arn:aws:codecommit:region:account-id:repository-name)
        - install Codedeploy agent
     CodeDeploy timeouts
       - A build in a queue that does not start after the number of minutes specified in its time out value is removed from the queue.
       - The default timeout value is eight hours.
       - You can override the build queue timeout with a value between 5 minutes and 8 hours when you run your build.
       - By setting the timeout configuration, the build process will automatically terminate post the expiry of the configured timeout.
     CodeDeploy Artifacts
       - Artifacts are the files that are worked on by actions in the pipeline, such as files or folders with application
         code, index page files, scripts, and so on
       - Codedeploy artifacts are stored in user specified S3 bucker

  CodeDeploy AppSpec File
    Configuration file
      - Defines the parameters to be used during CodeDeploy deployment, e.g. OS, files,
        [lifecycle event] hooks
    appspec.yml
      - should be saved in the root of your revision
      - revision/deployment folder contains:
         appspec.yml <-- must be placed in the root of the directory of your revision else deployment will fail
         /Scripts    <-- Scripts be run directory
         /Config     <-- Any configuration files directory
         /Source     <-- Application source code directory
    hooks
      - lifecycle event hooks have a very specific run order
      - scripts you want to run a specific lifecycle events during deployment
      - The hooks section for an EC2/On-Premises deployment contains mappings that link deployment
        lifecycle event hooks to one or more scripts.
      - The hooks section for a Lambda deployment specifies Lambda validation functions to run during a
        deployment lifecycle event.
    Format:
      EC2/on-premise:
        - AppSpec files on an EC2/on-premises compute platform must be a YAML-formatted file named appspec.yml and
          it must be placed in the root of the directory structure of an application's source code.
      Lambda
        - YAML and JSON supported
        - File structure depends on whether you are deploying to Lambda or EC2

  CodeDeploy Appspec File Reference:
    https://docs.aws.amazon.com/codedeploy/latest/userguide/reference-appspec-file.html
  Example EC2 appspec.yml file
     https://docs.aws.amazon.com/codedeploy/latest/userguide/reference-appspec-file-example.html

    version: 0.0
    os: linux                                            <---- Operating System
    files:
      - source: Config/config.txt                        <----- Files to copy during deployment
        destination: /webapps/Config
      - source: Source
        destination: /webapps/myApp
    hooks:                                               <------ Scripts you want run at each stage of they deployment
      BeforeInstall:
        - location: Scripts/UnzipResourceBundle.sh
        - location: Scripts/UnzipDataBundle.sh
      AfterInstall:
        - location: Scripts/RunResourceTests.sh
          timeout: 180
      ApplicationStart:
        - location: Scripts/RunFunctionalTests.sh
          timeout: 3600
      ValidateService:
        - location: Scripts/MonitorService.sh
          timeout: 3600
          runas: codedeployuser

  CodeDeploy Lifecycle Event Hooks
    Run Order
      - Lifecycle event hooks are run in a specific order known as the 'Run Order'
    In-Place Deployments
      Broadly 3 Phases:
        - de-registering from a load balancer
        - installation
        - re-registering with a Load Balancer
    Logical Flow
      - For the exam you'll need to understand the logical flow
      e.g. Installation Phase 2:
        ApplicationStop -> DownloadBundle -> BeforeInstall -> Install -> AfterInstall ->
            Application Start -> ValidateService

  CodeDeploy Agent
    - The AWS CodeDeploy agent is a software package that, when installed and configured on an instance,
      makes it possible for that instance to be used in CodeDeploy deployments
    - The CodeDeploy agent is required only if you deploy to an EC2/On-Premises compute platform.
    - The agent is not required for deployments that use the ECS or Lambda
    - archives revisions and log files on instances, and cleans up these artifacts to conserve disk space
    EC2 permissions for CodeDeploy
     - During CodeDeploy deployment, the EC2 instance accesses CodeCommit using the permissions defined in its instance profile role;
       therefore, it is the EC2 instance needs CodeCommit access. The specific CodeCommit permission needed to pull code is codecommit:GitPull.

  CodeDeploy Deployment Groups
    - You can specify one or more deployment groups for a CodeDeploy application.
    - Can be used deploy application to different sets of EC2 instances at different times
    - The deployment group contains settings and configurations used during the deployment.
    - Most deployment group settings depend on the compute platform used by your application.
    - Some settings, such as rollbacks, triggers, and alarms can be configured for deployment groups for any compute platform.

9.29 Developer Theory Summary - Part 2

  Overview of Deployment Options on AWS AWS Whitepaper
  https://docs.aws.amazon.com/whitepapers/latest/overview-deployment-options/introduction.html
  -> PDF

  Practicing Continuous Integration and Continuous Delivery on AWS
  https://docs.aws.amazon.com/whitepapers/latest/practicing-continuous-integration-continuous-delivery/welcome.html


  CodeArtifact
    - an artifact repository makes it easy for developers to find the software packages they need
    Package
      - a bundle of software used in your software development process
    Approved Package
      - Developers can find approved packages and can also publish their own
    External Connections to 3rd party repos
      - Create upstream repository with an external connection to pull packages from an external
        repository (e.g. the npm Registry for Node.js packages)


  CodeArtifact Demo
    - if you want to pull packages from an external repository, create an upstream repository with
      an external connection to the public repository (e.g. the npm registy for Node.js packages)

      my-domain
      |-------------------------------------------|
      |                           Upstream        |
      | Repository                Repository      | External    Public Repo
      | |-----------|           |-------------|   | Connection  |-------------|
      | |my-repo    | --------> | npm-store   |   | ----------> | npm Registry|
      | |           | <-------- |             |   | <---------- |             |
      | |           |           |             |   |             |-------------|
      | |-----------|           |-------------|   |
      |      ^                                    |
      |------|------------------------------------|
             | CodeArtifact Domain
             |
           Developer

  CodePipeline
    Continuous Integration (CI) / Continuous Delivery (CD) Service
      - Orchestrates your end-to-end software release process based on a workflow you define
    Automated
      - automatically triggers your pipeline as soon as a change is detected in your source code repository
    Integrates with AWS & Third-Party Tools
      - CodeCommit, CodeBuild, CodeDeploy, Github, Jenkins, Elastic Beanstalk, CloudFormation,
        Lambda, Elastic Container Service (ECS)

  CodePipeline
    CI/CD
      - continuous Integration / Continuous Delivery
      - orchestrates the end-to-end software release process
    Automated
      - avoids manual deployments
      - automatically triggers your pipeline as soon as a change is detected in your source code repository
    Integrates with AWS & Third-Party Tools
      - CodeCommit, CodeBuild, CodeDeploy, Github (source control), Jenkins (build tool), Elastic Beanstalk,
        CloudFormation, Lambda, Elastic Container Service (ECS)
    Add test Stage
      - CodePipeline allows you to create a test stage with a test action prior to the deployment stage.


  Elastic Container Service (ECS)
    Containers
      - virtual operating environment with everything the software needs to run
      - includes libraries, system tools, code, and runtime
      - allows applications to be built using independent stateless components or microservices
        running in multiple containers
    ECS
      Container orchestration
      - ECS will run your containers on clusters of virtual machines
    Fargate
      Serverless
      - you don't need to worry about the underlying EC2 instances where your containers are running
    EC2
      - if you want to control the installation, configuration, and management of your compute environment
    Elastic Container Registry
      - container registery
      - this is where your can store your container images - Docker or Windows Containers
    Elastic Container Agent
      - ECS container agent allows container instances to connect to your cluster.
      - The Amazon ECS container agent is included in the Amazon ECS-optimized AMIs, but you can also
         install it on any Amazon EC2 instance that supports the Amazon ECS specification.
      - The Linux variants of the ECS-optimized AMI look for agent configuration data in the /etc/ecs/ecs.config
         file when the container agent starts
         - /etc/ecs/ecs.config file example:
             ECS_CLUSTER=MyCluster
             ECS_ENGINE_AUTH_TYPE=docker
             ECS_ENABLE_TASK_IAM_ROLE=<IAM role>
             ECS_ENGINE_AUTH_DATA={"https://index.docker.io/v1/":{"username":"my_name","password":"my_password","email":"email@example.com"}}
             ECS_LOGLEVEL=debug
    ECS CLI to create service
      aws ecs create-service --service-name ecs-simple-service --task-definition ecs-demo --desired-count 10
      - creates a new service in your default region called ecs-simple-service. The service uses the ecs-demo task
        definition and it maintains 10 instantiations of that task.
    ECS use CloudWatch Logs
      - Before your container instances can send log data to CloudWatch Logs, you must create an IAM policy to allow
        your container instances to use the CloudWatch Logs APIs, and then you must attach that policy to ecsInstanceRole.
      - This policy has one statement that grants permissions to create log groups and log streams, to upload log events
        to log streams, and to list details about log streams, e.g:
              { "Version": "2012-10-17", "Statement": [
                  { "Effect": "Allow", "Action": [ "logs:CreateLogGroup", "logs:CreateLogStream", "logs:PutLogEvents", "logs:DescribeLogStreams" ],
                          "Resource": [ "arn:aws:logs:*:*:*" ]
                   }
                ]
               }
      - Before your containers can send logs to CloudWatch, you must specify the awslogs log driver for containers in your
        task definition
      -  If you're using the Fargate launch type for your tasks, you need to add the required logConfiguration parameters
         to your task definition to turn on the awslogs log driver.

   Fargate Launch Types
      Single task definition
        - Using a single task definition allows the 2 or more containers to share resources
        - Containers share a common lifecycle (launched and terminated together)
        - containers that require running on the same underlying host (e.g. one container references the other
          on a localhost port)
      Multiple task definition
        - else, you should define your containers in separate task definitions that so they can scale, provision,
          and deprovision them separately

   CloudFormation
     Infrastructure As Code
       - cloudformation allows you to manage, configure, and provision AWS infrastructure as
         YAML or JSON code
     Template main sections
       Parameters:
         - input custom values
       Conditions:
         - e.g. provision resources based on environment
         - define conditions that are applied to Resources to determine if they should be created
         - Conditions cannot be used within the Parameters section.
       Resources
         - this section is mandatory and describes the AWS resources that CloudFormation will create
       Mappings
         - allows you to create custom mappings like mapping AMIs to Regions
       Transform
         - allows your to reference code located in S3, e.g. Lambda code, or reusable
           snippets of CloudFormation code
         - can also be used to specify the application of the Serverless Application Model (SAM)
       Outputs
         - specify output values

   CloudFormation Parameter Types:
     String                              : A literal string
     Number                              : An integer or float
     List<Number>                        : An array of integers or floats
     CommaDelimitedList                  : An array of literal strings that are separated by commas
     AWS::EC2::KeyPair::KeyName          : An Amazon EC2 key pair name
     AWS::EC2::SecurityGroup::Id         : A security group ID
     AWS::EC2::Subnet::Id                : A subnet ID
     AWS::EC2::VPC::Id                   : A VPC ID
     List<AWS::EC2::VPC::Id>             : An array of VPC IDs
     List<AWS::EC2::SecurityGroup::Id>   : An array of security group IDs
     List<AWS::EC2::Subnet::Id>          : An array of subnet IDs

   CloudFormation Puesdo Parameters (return values):
     AWS::AccountID          returns AWS Account ID is a 12-digit number
     AWS::NotificationARNs   returns the list of SNS ARNs to which your stack related events are published
     AWS::NoValue            used to tell CloudFormation to "do nothing". Does not return a value, thus ending
                               the execution of that branch of logic.
     AWS::Region             returns the AWS region
     AWS::Partition          returns partition (Public Partition: aws, AWS GovCloud: aws-us-gov, AWS China: aws-cn)
     AWS::StackName          returns stack name
     AWS::StackID            returns stack ID
     AWS::URLSuffix          returns the region-specific domain for your environment

   CloudFormation Builtin functions
     !GetAtt - The Fn::GetAtt intrinsic function returns the value of an attribute from a resource in the template.
     !Sub    - The intrinsic function Fn::Sub substitutes variables in an input string with values that you specify.
     !Ref     - The intrinsic function Fn:Ref returns the value of the specified parameter or resource.
     !FindInMap - The intrinsic function Fn::FindInMap returns the value corresponding to keys in a two-level map that
                  is declared in the Mappings section

   CloudFormation Deployment packages AWS::Lambda::Function
     Container image:
       - To deploy a function defined as a container image, you specify the location of a container image
          in the ECR registry in the  AWS::Lambda::Function block.
     zip file deployment package
        - You can upload all the code as a zip deployment package to S3 and refer the S3 object in AWS::Lambda::Function block
     Inline code
        - For Node.js and Python functions, you can specify the function code inline in the CloudFormation template.
        - Write the AWS Lambda code inline in CloudFormation in the AWS::Lambda::Function block as long as
          there are no third-party dependencies

   CloudFormation Demo
     YAML or JSON Template
       - YAML or JSoN templates are used to describe the end state of the infrastructure you are
         either provisioning or changing
     S3
       - after creating the template, you upload it to CloudFormation where it is stored in S3
     API Calls
       - CloudFormation reads the template and makes API calls on your behalf
     CloudFormation Stack
       - the resulting set of resources that CloudFormation builds from your template is called a "stack"

     CloudFormation rolling back
        https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/aws/new-for-aws-cloudformation-quickly-retry-stack-operations-from-the-point-of-failure/
        - you can roll back to previous state and delete the entire stack as well
       stack creatation or update failures:
          - When such an error occurs, CloudFormation rolls back the stack to the previous stable condition.
          - For a stack creation, that means deleting all resources created up to the point of the error.
          - For a stack update, it means restoring the previous configuration.
        Stack Failure options
          - To preserve successfully provisioned resources with the AWS CLI, use the "--disable-rollback" flag with
            create-stack/update-stack/execute-change-set CLI commands
          - In the CloudFormation console, for Stack failure options, select "Preserve successfully provisioned resources"
            to keep, in case of errors, the resources that have already been created. Failed resources are always rolled back
            to the last known stable state.
        Rollback paused (failure with "Preserve successfully provisioned resources" enabled) options:
          - Retry: To retry the stack operation without any change.
          - Update: To update the template or the parameters before retrying the stack creation. The stack update starts
                    from where the last operation was interrupted by an error.
          - Rollback: To roll back to the last known stable state. This is similar to default CloudFormation behavior.

  CloudFormation CLI
    aws cloudformation package:  packages the local artifacts (local paths) that your AWS CloudFormation template references.
                                   The command will upload local artifacts, such as your source code for your AWS Lambda function
    aws cloudformation deploy:   deploys the specified AWS CloudFormation template by creating and then executing a changeset.

  CloudFormation with CodePipeline flow example
    - CloudFormation and CodePipeline, you can use continuous delivery to automatically build and test changes to your
      AWS CloudFormation templates before promoting them to production stacks.
    Stage 1: Source
      - The source code is located in an S3 bucket and is identified by its file name. Bucket Versioning must be enabled
    Stage 2: TestStage
      - the pipeline creates the test stack, waits for approval, and then deletes the test stack.
    Stage 3: ProdStage
      - The ProdStage stage of the pipeline creates a change set against the existing production stack, waits for approval, and then
        executes the change set.

  Serverless Application Model (SAM)
    - define and provision serverless applications using CloudFormation
    - an open-source framework for building serverless applications
    - framework that is used to deploy serverless infrastructure as code
    - SAM has its own CLI to package and deploy its code
      Sam package
         - packages your application and uploads a deployment file to S3
         - sam package obsolete command, where "sam deploy" now includes the "sam package" steps
      sam deploy
        - deploys your serverless app using CloudFormation
        -  zips your application artifacts, uploads them either to S3 (for .zip file archives) or to ECR (for container images).
           The command then deploys your application to the AWS Cloud.
     Cloudformation template Transform section
        - The Transform section specifies one or more macros that CloudFormation uses to process your template. The
          Transform section builds on the simple, declarative language of AWS CloudFormation with a powerful macro
          system. The declaration "Transform: AWS::Serverless-2016-10-31" is required for AWS SAM template files.

  AWS SAM Template Example:

     AWSTemplateFormatVersion: '2010-09-09'
     Transform: 'AWS::Serverless-2016-10-31'
     Resources:
       MyFunction:
         Type: 'AWS::Serverless::Function'        <----- Creates a Lambda function, IAM execution role, and event source mappings
         Properties:                                     which trigger the function
           Handler: index.handler                 <----- Function within your code that is called to begin execution.
           Runtime: nodejs6.10                    <----- The runtime environment (language that function is written).
           CodeUri: 's3://my-bucket/function.zip' <----- the S3 object this Uri references MUST be a Lambda deployment package.
                                                         Either CodeUri or InlineCode must be specified.
  AWS Serverless Application Repository (SAR)
   - SAR is a managed repository for serverless applications
   Templates
     - define whole applications via AWS SAM (Serverless Application Model - CloudFormation) templates
     - private by default
   Publish or Deploy
     - publish your own applications
     - deploy publicly available applications or shared with your account
   AWS Lambda
     - heavily integrated with AWS Lambda

  SAM API
    AWS::Serverless::Api
      - Creates a collection of Amazon API Gateway resources and methods that can be invoked through HTTPS endpoints
    AWS::Serverless::Application
      - Embeds a serverless application from the AWS Serverless Application Repository or from an Amazon S3 bucket as a nested application
    AWS::Serverless::Connector
      - Configures permissions between two resources
    AWS::Serverless::Function
      - Creates an AWS Lambda function, an IAM execution role, and event source mappings that trigger the function
    AWS::Serverless::GraphQLApi
      - create and configure an AWS AppSync GraphQL API for your serverless application
    AWS::Serverless::HttpApi
      - Creates an Amazon API Gateway HTTP API
    AWS::Serverless::LayerVersion
      - Creates a Lambda LayerVersion that contains library or runtime code needed by a Lambda Function.
    AWS::Serverless::SimpleTable
      - Creates a DynamoDB table with a single attribute primary key.
    AWS::Serverless::StateMachine
      - Creates an AWS Step Functions state machine

  CloudFormation Nested Stacks
    Code Re-use
      - allows you to re-use your CloudFormation code so you don't need to copy/paste every time
    Common Use Cases - Frequently Used Configs
      - Useful for frequently used configurations, e.g. load balancers, web or application servers
    Template Stored in S3
      - Create CloudFormation template, store it in S3, and then reference it in the cloudFormation
        template Resources section
    Stack Resource Type
      - Reference the nested stack in the Resource section of any CloudFormation template using "Stack"
        resource type

         Resources:
         Type: AWS::CloudFormation::Stack

  CloudFormation prevent resources from getting deleted or updated:
    Deletion Policy:
      - set the DeletionPolicy attribute to prevent the deletion of an individual resource at the stack level.
      - With the DeletionPolicy attribute you can preserve, and in some cases, backup a resource when its stack is deleted
      Deletion Policy Settings
         Delete:
           - deletes the the resource and all it contents if applicable during stack deletion
           - If a resource has no DeletionPolicy attribute, AWS CloudFormation deletes the resource by default.
             expect Snapshot is the default for AWS::RDS::DBCluster resources and for AWS::RDS::DBInstance resources,
         Retain:
           - To keep a resource when its stack is deleted
         RetainExceptOnCreate:
           - behaves like Retain for stack operations, except for the stack operation that initially created the resource
         Snapshot:
           - For resources that support snapshots, such as AWS::EC2::Volume, specify Snapshot
             to have CloudFormation create a snapshot before deleting the resource.
    IAM policies
       -  IAM policies to restrict the ability of users to delete or update a stack and its resources.
    Stack Policy
       - Assign a stack policy to prevent updates to stack resources.
       - A stack policy is a JSON document that defines the update actions that can be performed on designated resources
    Termination Protection
        - Turn on termination protection to prevent users from deleting the stack from the CloudFormation
          console or CLI.

  CloudFormation Change Sets
    - Change sets allow you to preview how proposed changes to a stack might impact your running resources, for example,
      whether your changes will delete or replace any critical resources
      Change set steps
        1. Create Change set
          - Create a change set by submitting changes for the stack that you want to update.
          - You can submit a modified stack template or modified input parameter values.
        2. View Change set
          - View the change set to see which stack settings and resources will change
        3. Optional: Create additional Change sets
        4. Execute the Change Set
          - CloudFormation updates your stack with those change

  CloudFormation Drift Detection
    - Drift detection enables you to detect whether a stack's actual configuration differs, or has drifted, from
      its expected configuration. Use CloudFormation to detect drift on an entire stack, or individual resources
      within the stack.
    - A resource is considered to have drifted if any of its actual property values differ from the expected property values
      as defined in the stack template and any values specified as template parameters

  Exporting CloudFormation Stack Values
    template outputs:
         - Use the Outputs section to define variables that you would like to export to other
             cloudFormation templates
         - below example is exporting the SubnetID and SecurityGroupID:

         "Outputs" : {
          "PublicSubnet" : {
            "Description" : "The subnet ID to use for public web servers",
            "Value" :  { "Ref" : "PublicSubnet" },
            "Export" : { "Name" : {"Fn::Sub": "${AWS::StackName}-SubnetID" }}
          },
          "WebServerSecurityGroup" : {
            "Description" : "The security group ID to use for public web servers",
            "Value" :  { "Fn::GetAtt" : ["WebServerSecurityGroup", "GroupId"] },
            "Export" : { "Name" : {"Fn::Sub": "${AWS::StackName}-SecurityGroupID" }}
          }

  Importing CloudFormation Stack Values

     template ImportValue fucntion:
       - Use "ImportValue" function to import values
       - the name of the stack providing the import values is provided as a parameter:

        "Fn::ImportValue": {
                    "Fn::Sub": "${NetworkStackParameter}-SubnetID"
        "Fn::ImportValue": {
                    "Fn::Sub": "${NetworkStackParameter}-SecurityGroupID"

  Exporting/Importing stack values limitations:
    - Exported Output Values in CloudFormation must have unique names within a single Region"
    - You can use the intrinsic function Fn::ImportValue to import only values that have been exported within the same region
    - You can't delete a stack if another stack references one of its outputs.
    - You can't modify or remove an output value that is referenced by another stack.

   CloudFormation StackSets
     - cloudFormation StackSets extends the capability of stacks by enabling you to create, update, or delete stacks
       across multiple accounts and AWS Regions with a single operation.
     - Using an administrator account, you define and manage an AWS CloudFormation template, and use the template
       as the basis for provisioning stacks into selected target accounts across specified AWS Regions
     - without stacksets, using CloudFormation in multiple regions, you need to define template in each region

  Introducing Amplify
    Frontend Developers
      - create full-stack web and mobile applications on AWS
    Amplify Libraries
      - integrate with Cognito, S3, Lambda, and API Gateways to create a reliable backend
    Amplify Services
      Amplify Hosting
        - Static web hosting that integrates with your code repository for CI/CD functionality
      Amplify Studio
        - Simple visual tool
        - Used to configure the frontend and backend of your application

  Amplify Demo
    Hosting
      - Amplify Hosting is used for hosting web applications
    Repository
      - you can upload your code to a repository like GitHub, Bitbucket, or CodeCommit
    Continuous Deployment
      - amplify will deploy your code directly from the repository every time a code change is detected

  Cloud Development Kit (CDK)
    Deploy
       Deploy applications and AWS resources
         - use a programming language of your choice (TypeScript, Python, Java, .NET, and Go)
    Define
       Apps, Stacks, and Constructs
          App:   Container for one or more stacks
          Stack: Unit of deployment (similar to a cloudformation stack)
                 - all resources in a stack a provisioned as a single unit
          Contruct: Defines the AWS resources
                  - a stack includes 1 or more constructs
          CDK Toolkit (also known as the CDK CLI): The toolkit is used to create and deploy using the CDK
    Synthesize
      - CDK can synthesize the code to a CloudFormation template
      - Use CDK CLI (toolkit) to create CloudFormation template from your code and deploy it

     CDK Process
        Create a New CDK Project
          - run "cdk init" inside an empty directory
        Compile the App (e.g. typescript compile:)
          - use "npm run build" to compile the app
        Synthesize [cloudformation template]
          - use "cdk synth" to create a CloudFormation template
        Deploy the Stack
          - use "cdk deploy" to deploy the stack using CloudFormation

          cdk init ----> npm run build ------> cdk synth ------> cdk deploy

9.30 AWS Certified Developer - Associate - Developers Theory Quiz

Question 3
In the CodeDeploy AppSpec file, what are hooks used for?

Choices:
 - Hooks are reserved for future use
 - To specify code, scripts or functions that you want to run at set points in the deployment lifecycle <- correct answer
 - To reference AWS resources that will be used during the deployment
 - To specify files that you want to copy during the deployment

Correct. The hooks section specifies the location (as relative paths starting from the root of the revision bundle) of the scripts
to run during each phase of the deployment. Each phase of a deployment is called a deployment lifecycle event.


Question 14
When deploying application code to Lambda, the AppSpec file can be written in which language?

Choose 2
 - XML
 - Python
 - JSON        <- correct answer
 - YAML        <- correct answer

Correct. The application specification file (AppSpec file) is a YAML-formatted or JSON-formatted file used by CodeDeploy to
         manage a deployment.
Note: CodeDeploy Lambda AppSpec can be either JSON or Lambda. However, EC2 and on-premise only support YAML appspec file.

Question 19

How can you prevent AWS CloudFormation from deleting successfully provisioned resources during a stack create operation,
while allowing resources in a failed state to be updated or deleted upon the next stack operation?

Choose 2
 - Set Termination Protection to Enabled in the CloudFormation console
 - Use the --enable-termination-protection flag with the AWS CLI
 - Use the "--disable-rollback" flag with the AWS CLI                                                              <-- correct answer
 - In the CloudFormation console, for Stack failure options, select "Preserve successfully provisioned resources"  <--- correct answer


This AWS CloudFormation console "Termination Protection" option keeps the stack unchanged if a user attempts to delete it.

This AWS CLI "--enable-termination-protection" option keeps the stack unchanged if a user attempts to delete it.

Correct Answer Info:
Specifying the disable-rollback option or on-failure DO_NOTHING enumeration during a create-stack operation will preserve
successfully provisioned resources during a stack create operation.

Create operations set to "Preserve successfully provisioned resources" preserves the state of successful resources,
while failed resources will stay in a failed state until the next update operation is performed.

Stack failure option
https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AWSCloudFormation/latest/UserGuide/stack-failure-options.html#stack-failure-options-console


Question 24

You are running your application using Docker provisioned with Elastic Beanstalk. You would like to upgrade the application to a new version. How should you approach this?

Choices:
 - Bundle your Dockerfile and the application code into a zip file, then upload and deploy it using the Elastic Beanstalk console. <- Correct answer
 - Create a new Docker image using the latest code, deploy it using Elastic Beanstalk, then terminate your old environment.
 - Create a new Docker image using the latest code, log in to the underlying EC2 instance and install the new Docker image.
 - Bundle your code into a zip file, upload and deploy it using Cloud Formation.


Elastic Beanstalk supports the deployment of web applications as Docker containers. Each time you upload a new version of
your application with the Elaatic Beanstalk console or the EB CLI, Elastic Beanstalk creates an application version. When
using the console you can choose to upload and deploy your code in a single step. Elastic Beanstalk will manage the previous
version of your code so you can revert back to it if necessary.

  Chapter 10 Advanced IAM

10.1 Web Identity Federation

  About web identity federation
  https://docs.aws.amazon.com/IAM/latest/UserGuide/id_roles_providers_oidc.html

  Web Identify Federation
    - Simplifies authentication and authorization for web applications
    User Access to AWS Resources
      - users access to AWS resources after successfully authentication with a web-based identity
        provider like Facebook, Amazon, and Google
    Authentication
      - following successful authentication, user receive an authentication code from the web ID provider
    Authorization
      - User can trade this authentication code for temporary AWS security credentials, authorizing
        access to AWS resources

   Web ID Federation with Amazon Cognito
     Amazon Cognito
       - provides web ID federation including sign-up and sign-in functionality for your
         applications, and access for guest users
     Acts as an Identity Broker
       - manages authentication between your application and web ID providers (e.g. Facebook), so
         you don't need to write any additional code
     Multiple Devices
       - Synchronizes user data for multiple devices
     Recommended for Mobile
       - recommended for all mobile applications that call AWS services

  Cognito Use Case
    Web Identity Federation using Social Media Accounts like Facebook
      - mobile shopping application using S3 for product data and dynamoDB for customer data
      - customer needs to access the product data on S3 and their own customer data in dynamoDB table(s)

      Facebook
        ^ |
        | |  authentiation
        | V                                        AWS Resources
      |------| ------> Cognito                    |--------------|
      | User | <-----                             | EC2          |
      |      |       Cognito temporaray access    |     DynamoDB |
      |------| ---------------------------------> | S3           |
                                                  |--------------|

  Authentication with Amazon Cognito
     Temporary Credentials
       - Cognito brokers between the app and Facebook, Amazon, or Google to provide temporary credentials
     IAM Role
       - the temporary credentials map to an IAM role, allowing access to the required resources
     Secure and Seamless
       - no need for the application to embed or store AWS credentials locally on the device

  Cognito User Pools and Identity Pools
    User Pools
      - user directories used to manage sign-up and sign-in functionality for mobile and web applications
      Sign-in
        - Users can sign-in directly to the User Pool, or using Web Identity provider like Facebook,
          Amazon, or Google
    Identify Pools
      - Identity pools enable you to provide temporary AWS credentials
      - enabling access to AWS services like S3 or DynamoDB


  Cognito User Pool and Identity Pool Workflow
       - after Cognito successful brokers sign-in with Facebook, Facebook will return a JWT (JSON Web Token) Token
       - JWT is exchanged for AWS credentials by the Cognito Identity Pool
       - AWS Credentials map to IAM AssumeRole
       - IAM Assume Role gives user access to AWS resources

               JWT     Cognito
      User <---------> User Pool <-----------> Facebook
       \  \
        \  \  AWS Credentials
         \  \-------------------> Cognito Identity Pool
          \      IAM AssumeRole
           \---------------------> S3 / AWS resources


  Cognito Push Synchronization
    - synchronization across multiple devices
    Devices
      - Cognito tracks the association between user identity and the various different devices they sign-in from
    Seamless using Push Synchronization
      - Cognito uses Push Synchronizaion and synchronizes user data across multiple devices
    SNS Silent Notification
      - SNS Notification to all the devices associated with a giver user identity whenever data stored
        in the cloud changes

  Push Synchronization Using SNS
    example:
     - User changes his address from mobile phone

                                    |----> tablet
       User -------->   SNS   ------|
             New      Silent Push   |----> mobile phone
             address  Notification

  Exam Tips

     Web Identity Federation
       - allows users to authentication with a Web Identity Provider like Google, Facebook or Amazon
       - following a successful authentication, an authentication token (JWT token) is exchanged for
         a temporary AWS credentials, allowing users to assume an IAM role, with permissions to access
         AWS resources

     Web Identity Federation
       Cognito
         - Cognito acts as an Identity Broker, handling all interactions with web identity providers
         User Sign-up
           - Cognito provides sign-up, sign-in, and guest user access
         Best Practices for Mobile Apps
           - Cognito is the AWS recommended approach for web ID federation for mobile applications


    Cognito User Pools and Identity Pools
      User Pools
        - user directories used to manage sign-up and sign-in functionality for mobile and web applications
      Identify Pools
        - Identity pools enable you to provide temporary AWS credentials
        - enabling access to AWS services like S3 or DynamoDB

  Cognito Push Synchronization Using SNS
    - Cognito push synchronozation uses SNS to send silent push notification of user data updates to
      multiple devices associated with a single user ID
      - example: user updates address on mobile phone, and updated address needs to be seen also on tablet

10.4 Cognito Identity Pools Demo - Part 2


  3. Create a Cognito Identity Pool and an IAM Role
    - to enable unauthenticated entities to access the DynamoDB Table

   # In the CloudShell, create new identity pool, named DynamoPool, allow unauthenticated entities:

    $ aws cognito-identity create-identity-pool \
      --identity-pool-name DynamoPool \
      --allow-unauthenticated-identities \
      --output json

    # Download the permissions policy for the role we are going to create.

    $ wget https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ACloudGuru-Resources/course-aws-certified-developer-associate/main/Cognito_Demo/myCognitoPolicy.json

    # Copy the ID of the identity pool from output of  "aws congito-identity create-identity-pool ..." command
    # update the myCognitoPolicy.json with the ID of the identity pool you just created.


    # Create an IAM role named Cognito_DynamoPoolUnauth using "myCognitoPolicy.json" policy doc:

    $ aws iam create-role --role-name Cognito_DynamoPoolUnauth --assume-role-policy-document file://myCognitoPolicy.json --output json

    # Grant the Cognito_DynamoPoolUnauth role read access to DynamoDB by attaching a managed policy
    #   (AmazonDynamoDBReadOnlyAccess).

    $ aws iam attach-role-policy --policy-arn arn:aws:iam::aws:policy/AmazonDynamoDBReadOnlyAccess --role-name Cognito_DynamoPoolUnauth


    # Verify attach-role-policy command by Getting the IAM role Amazon Resource Name (ARN).
    $ aws iam get-role --role-name Cognito_DynamoPoolUnauth --output json

    # Add our role to the Cognito Identity Pool. Replace the identity pool ID with your own identity pool ID
    #  and use the role ARN from the previous step.

aws cognito-identity set-identity-pool-roles \
--identity-pool-id "us-east-1:4f043a32-f95f-4482-a9c7-d99b494caa71" \
--roles unauthenticated=arn:aws:iam::033115095740:role/Cognito_DynamoPoolUnauth --output json

   # Verify role was added to our identity pool - Replace the identity pool ID with your own identity pool ID:

   $ aws cognito-identity get-identity-pool-roles  --identity-pool-id "us-east-1:4f043a32-f95f-4482-a9c7-d99b494caa71"
   # expected output:
   {
    "IdentityPoolId": "us-east-1:4f043a32-f95f-4482-a9c7-d99b494caa71",
    "Roles": {
        "unauthenticated": "arn:aws:iam::033115095740:role/Cognito_DynamoPoolUnauth"
    }
   }


  4. Configure our Website
   - add the details of our Cognito identity pool to our web page

  -> instance Connect window
  # We can now specify the Cognito credentials in our application - i.e. in the JavaScript section of our webpage!
  # Replace the identity pool ID with your own and the role ARN with your own role ARN.
  # We are going to add this snippet (replace exiting snippet) to our index.html:

  $ sudo vi /var/www/html

  #  update the the following code in the 'index.html'
  AWS.config.credentials = new AWS.CognitoIdentityCredentials({
  IdentityPoolId: "us-east-1:2344c8f8-bc7d-4bf8-9836-c8be99916812",
  RoleArn: "arn:aws:iam::xxxxxxxxxxxx:role/Cognito_DynamoPoolUnauth"


   -> verify Web Server is running using instance Public IP -> click on "Click here to learn an awesome fact"
     -> should return facts from DynamoDB table


  Exam Tips

    Cognito Identity Pools
      - Create temporary AWS credentials with limited privileges, allowing applications to access
        AWS services
      - for example, allowing anonymous user accessing a website to read data in a DynamoDB table

---------------------------------------------------
10.5 Inline Policies vs Managed Policies vs Customer Managed Policies

  Identity Access Management (IAM)
    - used to define user access permissions with AWS
    3 Types of IAM Policies
      AWS Managed Policies
      Customer Managed Policies
      Inline Policies


  AWS Managed Policies
    - an IAM policy created and administered by AWS
    - provide policies for common use cases, e.g.
         AmazonDynamoDBFullAccess, AWSCodeCommitPowerUser, AmazonEC2ReadOnlyAccess
    - allow you to assign appropriate permissions to your users without having to
      write the policy yourself
    - can be attached to multiple users, groups, or roles in the saem AWS account
      or across different accounts
    - you cannot change the permissions defined in an AWS managed policy

  Customer Managed Policies
    created by you
      - a standalone policy that you create and administer inside your own AWS account
      - You can attach the policy to multiple users, groups, and roles only within your own acccount
    Copy an Existing Policy
      - in order to create a customer managed policy, you can copy and existing AWS managed
        policy and customize it to fit the requirements of your organization
    Your Needs
      - Recommended for use cases where the existing AWS managed policies don't meet the
        needs of your environment

  Inline Policies
    1:1 Relationship
      - there is a strict 1:1 relationship between the entity and the policy
      - policy is embedded within the user, group, or role which it is applied
    Embedded
      - when you delete the user, group, or role in which the inline policy is embedded,
        the policy will also be deleted
    AWS recommendation
      - in most cases, AWS recommends using managed policies over inline policies
    Single User, Group, or Role
      - Useful when the policy must not be inadvertently assigned to any other user, group,
        or role than the one for which it is intended
      - Userful when The policy must only ever be attached to a single user, group, or role

    -> Console -> IAM -> Policy -> in filter, left click from Properties menu with "Type", "Path" and "Used as",
      select "Type"

    # to create an in-line policy for a user:
    -> Console -> IAM -> User -> select user -> Permissions <tab> -> Permission Policy -> Add Permissions ->
       create inline policy -> select a service -> give appropriate access ->  Name -> create

  Exam Tips

    Inline Policies vs Managed Policies vs Customer Managed Policies
      AWS Managed Policies
        - includes AWS managed default polices like: AmazonDynamoDBFullAccess,
           AWSCodeCommitPowerUser, AmazonEC2ReadOnlyAccess
        - can be applied to multiple users, groups, or roles
      Customer Managed Policies
        - created and managed by you
        - can be applied to multiple users, groups, or roles
      Inline Policies
        - managed by you and embedded in a single user, group, or role
        - there is a strict 1:1 relationship between the entity and the policy
      AWS recommendation
        - in most cases, AWS recommends using managed policies over inline policies

---------------------------------------------------
10.6 Working With AWS Managed Policies, Customer Managed Policies, and Inline Policies


About this lab
In this lab, we'll explore and work with the different types of IAM policies available: AWS managed policies,
customer managed policies, and inline policies. An AWS managed policy is a standalone policy that is created
and administered by AWS. A customer managed policy is a standalone policy that you administer in your own
AWS account. An inline policy is a policy that's embedded in an IAM identity (a user, group, or role).

Learning objectives
 Already exists:
   - 3 Users: Charlie, Sally, & Ian
 - Create a Customer Managed Policy
    - MyCustomerManagedPolicy policy with read access to DynamoDB

 - Attach the Correct Policies to the Users
   - attach MyCustomerManagedPolicy to Charlie
   - attach AWS Managed Policy, AWSLambdaFullAccess, to Sally
   - attach a new Inline Policy To Ian

 - Attach Your Customer Managed Policy to Sally
   - attach MyCustomerManagedPolicy to Charlie

  - Attempt to Attach Inline Policy to Sally (will not work)

---------
Create a Customer Managed Policy

    From the AWS console, use the top search bar to search for and navigate to IAM.
    From the left IAM dashboard menu, select Users.

        Note: In addition to the cloud_user, there should be 3 users: charlie, ian, and sally.

    From the left IAM dashboard menu, select Policies.
    Click Create Policy.
    In the Visual editor under Service, click Choose a service.
    In Service search field, search for DynamoDB.
    Select DynamoDB.
    In Actions, under Access level, select Read.
    In Resources, select All resources.
    Click Next:Tags > Next:Review.
    In the Name field, enter MyCustomerManagedPolicy.
    Click Create policy.
    Verify the policy was successfully created by filtering policies: Type > Type: Customer managed.

Attach the Correct Policies to the Users
Attach a Customer Managed Policy

    From the left menu, select Policies.
    Select the newly created MyCustomerManagedPolicy policy.
    Select the Policy usage tab, and click Attach.
    Select the charlie user, and click Attach policy.

Attach an AWS Managed Policy

    From the left menu, select Policies.
    In the Filter policies field, search for AWSLambda_FullAccess.
    Select the AWSLambda_FullAccess policy.
    At the top of the page, click Actions > Attach.
    Select the sally user, and click Attach policy.

Attach a New Inline Policy

    From the left menu, select Users.
    Select the ian user.
    In Permissions policies, click Add inline policy.
    In the Visual editor under Service, click Choose a service.
    In Service search field, search for S3.
    Select S3.
    In Actions, under Access level, select Read.
    In Resources, select All resources.
    Click Review policy.
    In the Name field, enter MyInlinePolicy.
    Click Create policy.

Attach Your Customer Managed Policy to Sally

    From the left menu, select Users.
    Select the sally user.
    Select the Permissions tab, and click Add permissions.
    Select Attach existing policies directly.
    Click Filter policies, and select Customer managed.
    Select the MyCustomerManagedPolicy policy.
    Click Next: Review > Add permissions.

Attempt to Attach an Inline Policy to Sally

    Click Add inline policy. You will be prompted to create a new policy. Click Cancel and try to see if you can add the policy via the ian user.
    From the left menu, select Users.
    Select the ian user.
    Select the MyInlinePolicy policy.

        Note: You won't see an option to select and attach it to another user. This is because inline policies are embedded within the user group or role they're attached to, so this inline policy is embedded within the ian user and we cannot attach it to another user like sally.

10.7 STS AssumeRoleWithWebIdentity

  AssumeRoleWithWebIdentity
  https://docs.aws.amazon.com/STS/latest/APIReference/API_AssumeRoleWithWebIdentity.html


  STS (Security Token Service) AssumeRoleWithWebIdentity
    - Security Token Service API Call
    STS API
      - assume-role-with-web-identity is an  API provide by the STS (Security Token Service)
    Temporary Credentials
      - Returns temporary security credentials for users authenticated by a mobile or web application
        or using a web ID provider like Amazon, Facebook, Google, etc
    Web Applications
      - regular web applications (when you are not using Cognito) can use the assume-role-with-web-identity API
      - for mobile applications, Cognito is recommended

  STS Example Workflow
     - User authenticates using with Web ID Provider, Facebook
     - When successfully authenticates with Facebook, it returns JWT (JSON Web Token)
     - application makes assume-role-with-web-identity API call to STS along with JWT
     - STS returns temporary AWS credentials
     - application uses AWS Credentials to access AWS Resources

               JWT
          User <--------------------  Facebook
          | ^ \ -------------------->
          |  \ \
          |   \ \  assume-role-with-web-identity
          |    \ \------------------------------> STS
          |     \---------------------------------
          \        AWS Credentials
           \
            \--------------> AWS Resources (S3, DynamoDB)


  Sample STS Response
     - the ARN identifier provided in <AssumeRoleUser> section of the response can be used if want to
       refer programmatically to the credentials
       - response includes a set or temporary credentials in the <Credentials> section
         - by default, secret access key and Access Key expire in 1 hour

    <AssumeRoleWebIdentityResponse  ......>
      <AssumeRoleWebIdentityResult>
        <SubjectFromWebIdentityToken ....>
        <Audience>client...
        <AssumedRoleUser>
          <Arn>arn:aws:sts::<account#>assumed-role/FederatedWebIdentityRole/app1</Arn>
          <AssumedRoleId>xxxxxxxxxxxxxxx:appl1</AssumedRoleId>
        </AssumedRoleUser>
        <Credentials>
          <SessionToken>xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx</SessionToken>
          <SecretAccessKey>xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx</SecretAccessKey>
          <Expiration>2020-10-24T23:00:23Z1</Expiration>
        </Credentials>
        <Provider>www.amazon.com</Provider
      </AssumeRoleWebIdentityResult>
       .  .  .
    </AssumeRoleWebIdentityResponse  ......>


  Exam Tips

    STS AssumeRoleWithWebIdentity
      STS
        Part of the Security Token Service
      Authentication
        - allows users who have authenticated with a web identity provider to access the AWS resources
      API Call
        - after the user has authenticated, the application makes the assum-role-with-web-identity API call
      Temporary Credentials
        -  if successful, STS will return temporary credentials enabling access to AWS resources
      AssumeRoleUser
        - within AssumedRoleUser, the Arn and AssumeRoleID are used to programmatically reference
          the temporary credentials, not an IAM role or user

10.8 Configuring Cross Account Access Demo

  IAM tutorial: Delegate access across AWS accounts using IAM roles
  https://docs.aws.amazon.com/IAM/latest/UserGuide/tutorial_cross-account-with-roles.html

  How to Enable Cross-Account Access to the AWS Management Console
  https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/security/how-to-enable-cross-account-access-to-the-aws-management-console/

  What is Cross-Account Access
    - delegated access to resources in different AWS accounts that you own
    Manage Resources in Other Accounts
      - Share resources in one account with users in a different account
    IAM Role
      - create a role in one account to allow access and grant permissions to users in a different account
    Switch Roles
      - switch roles in the AWS Management console. No password is required

  Example Use Case
      you have:
         - development (dev) account
         - production (prod) account
      give access:
         - to developers in dev account access to one S3 bucket in production account
           containing historical production data

  Demo
     - need to set up Developers IAM Group in dev account
     - in prod account, set up S3 "myprodbucket" bucket
     - in prod account, need IAM Policy with access to S3 bucket and attach it to IAM Role
       - this IAM Role will reference account ID of the dev account
     - IAM policy in dev account to allow assume the prod IAM role

     account IDs
             Prod (My account): 012345678901
             Dev (sandbox):     345678901234

   -> Dev -> console -> IAM -> User Groups -> Create Group ->  Name: Developers, Permissions: AdministratorAccess ->
      Create Group

   -> Dev -> console -> IAM -> Users -> Add User ->  User: Anna, Console access, pw: demo!Dev4A, no pw reset
     -> Next -> add to "developers" group -> Next -> Create User
     login with: https://345678901234.signin.aws.amazon.com/console

   -> Prod -> Console -> S3 -> create bucket -> Bucket Name: my-prod-share-data-pat
      -> Create Bucket

   -> Prod -> Console -> IAM -> create policy -> JSON:
             {
            "Version": "2012-10-17",
            "Statement": [
              {
                "Effect": "Allow",
                "Action": "s3:ListAllMyBuckets",
                "Resource": "arn:aws:s3:::*"
              },
              {
                "Effect": "Allow",
                "Action": [
                  "s3:ListBucket",
                  "s3:GetBucketLocation"
                 ],
                "Resource": "arn:aws:s3:::my-prod-share-data-pat"
              },
              {
                "Effect": "Allow",
                "Action": [
                  "s3:GetObject",
                  "s3:PutObject",
                  "s3:DeleteObject"
                ],
                "Resource": "arn:aws:s3:::my-prod-share-data-pat/*"
              }
            ]
          }
          -> Next -> Policy Name: developers-read-write-bucket -> create policy

   # attach policy to a role:
   -> Prod -> Console -> IAM -> create role -> Trusted entity type: AWS Account, Another AWS Account,
      Account ID ... -> Next -> permissions: developers-read-write-bucket policy -> Next ->
        Role Name: MyDeveloperAccess -> Create Role

   -> Dev -> Console -> IAM -> User Groups -> Developers -> Permissions -> Add Permissions
       ->  Create Inline Policy -> JSON ->
          {
              "Version": "2012-10-17",
              "Statement": {
                  "Effect": "Allow",
                  "Action": "sts:AssumeRole",
                  "Resource": "arn:aws:iam::PRODUCTION-ACCOUNT-ID:role/MyDeveloperAccess"
              }
          }
      -> Next -> Policy Name: AllowAssumesS3RoleInProd

    -> Login in the Dev account as Anna
       -> drop-down next to log name -> Switch Role ->
            Account <Prod ID>
            Role: MyDeveloperAccess
            -> Switch Role
            -> S3 -> shows prod bucket

  Exam Tips

   Steps to configure:
     - in dev account, create Developers IAM Group (DevGroup)
     - in dev account, create 'Anna' user and add her to up Developers IAM Group (DevGroup)
     - in prod account, create S3 "my-prod_shared-data" bucket
     - in prod account, create IAM Policy, dev-read-write-bucket, with access to S3 my-prod-shared-data
       bucket and attach it to IAM Role, prodShareDataRole
        - this IAM Role, devS3AccessRole, it will reference account ID of the dev account
     - in dev account, attach IAM Inline policy to DevGroup that allows it to assume the prod account IAM prodSharedDataRoek role
          Note: does not need to be Inline policy

10.9 Advanced IAM Summary

   Web Identity Federation
     - allows users to authentication with a Web Identity Provider like Google, Facebook or Amazon
     - following a successful authentication, an authentication token (JWT token) is exchanged for
       a temporary AWS credentials, allowing users to assume an IAM role, with permissions to access
       AWS resources
     Login & Access step Order
      - the user needs to authenticate with Facebook first, that will return a web identity token.
      - Then AWS STS is called and passes the web identity token as input.
      - AWS STS authorizes the call and provides temporary AWS access credentials. The user is allowed
      - to assume an IAM role and access AWS resources in accordance with the role's security policy.

  STS (Security Token Service) AssumeRoleWithWebIdentity
    STS
      Part of the Security Token Service
    Authentication
      - allows users who have authenticated with a web identity provider to access the AWS resources
    API Call
      - after the user has authenticated, the application makes the assum-role-with-web-identity API call
    Temporary Credentials
      -  if successful, STS will return temporary credentials enabling access to AWS resources
    AssumeRoleWithWebIdentity
      - sts assume-role-with-web-identity API calls can be used to enable a user authenticated by Facebook to access
        your web application hosted in AWS
      - assume-role-with-web-identity returns a set of temporary security credentials for users who have been
        authenticated in a mobile or web application with a web identity
    AssumeRoleUser
      - within AssumedRoleUser, the Arn and AssumeRoleID are used to programmatically reference
        the temporary credentials, not an IAM role or user


   Web Identity Federation - Cognito
     Cognito
       - Cognito acts as an Identity Broker, handling all interactions with web identity providers
       - Cognito works with external identity providers that support SAML or OpenID Connect, social identity
         providers (such as Facebook, Twitter, Amazon) and you can also integrate your own identity provider.
       User Sign-up
         - Cognito provides sign-up, sign-in, and guest user access
       Best Practices for Mobile Apps
         - Cognito is the AWS recommended approach for web ID federation for mobile applications


  Cognito Push Synchronization Using SNS
    - Cognito push synchronozation uses SNS to send silent push notification of user data updates to
      multiple devices associated with a single user ID
      - example: user updates address on mobile phone, and updated address needs to be seen also on tablet


  Cognito User Pool and Identity Pool Workflow
       - after Cognito successful brokers sign-in with Facebook, Facebook will return a JWT (JSON Web Token) Token
       - JWT is exchanged for AWS credentials by the Cognito Identity Pool
       - AWS Credentials map to IAM AssumeRole
       - IAM Assume Role gives user access to AWS resources

               JWT     Cognito
      User <---------> User Pool <-----------> Facebook
       \  \
        \  \  AWS Credentials
         \  \-------------------> Cognito Identity Pool
          \      IAM AssumeRole
           \---------------------> S3 / AWS resources

  Cognito User Pools and Identity Pools
    User Pools
      - user directories used to manage sign-up and sign-in functionality for mobile and web applications
      - User Pool provides authentication (who you are) as opposed to authorization (what you can do)
      Cognito Post authentication Lambda trigger
        - Because Cognito invokes this trigger after signing in a user, you can add custom logic after Cognito authenticates the user.
    Identify Pools
      - Identity pools enable you to provide temporary AWS credentials
      - enabling temporary access to AWS services like S3 or DynamoDB
      - identity pools enable you to create unique identities for your users and authenticate them with identity providers.
      - With an identity, you can obtain temporary, limited-privilege AWS credentials to access other AWS services
      - Think of User Pools as providing authentication, and Identity Pools as providing authorization.
      - Identity pools support anonymous guest users, as well as federation through third-party IdPs.
       Identity-based policy
         - You can create an identity-based policy that allows Cognito users to access objects in a specific S3 bucket.
         - This policy allows access only to objects with a name that includes Cognito, the name of the application,
           and the federated user's ID, represented by the ${cognito-identity.amazonaws.com:sub} variable.
           example:
           {
           . . .
              "Condition": {
             "StringLike": {
               "s3:prefix": [
                 "cognito/application-name/${cognito-identity.amazonaws.com:sub}/*"
               ]
           . . .
              "Resource": [
             "arn:aws:s3:::bucket-name/cognito/application-name/${cognito-identity.amazonaws.com:sub}/*"
             ]
          . . .
          }
    Identify Pools ALB integrations
      - Application Load Balancer can be used to securely authenticate users for accessing your applications.
      - This enables you to offload the work of authenticating users to your load balancer so that your applications
        can focus on their business logic
      - Note: there is no Cognito User Pool integration with CloudFront



  Inline Policies vs Managed Policies vs Customer Managed Policies
    AWS Managed Policies
      - includes AWS managed default polices like: AmazonDynamoDBFullAccess,
         AWSCodeCommitPowerUser, AmazonEC2ReadOnlyAccess
      - can be applied to multiple users, groups, or roles
      - AWS occasionally updates the permissions defined in an AWS managed policy
      - It is available for use by any AWS account
    Customer Managed Policies
      - created and managed by you
      - can be applied to multiple users, groups, or roles
    Inline Policies
      - managed by you and embedded in a single user, group, or role
      - there is a strict 1:1 relationship between the entity and the policy
      - when you deleted the user/group/role with the inline policy enbedded, the
        inline policy is also deleted
    AWS recommendation
      - in most cases, AWS recommends using managed policies over inline policies

  Configuring Cross-Account Access - Demo
     cross-account access
       https://docs.aws.amazon.com/IAM/latest/UserGuide/tutorial_cross-account-with-roles.html
       - allow a user from one AWS account to access and manage resources in another AWS accoun
       - you can delegate access to resources that are in different AWS accounts, and you don't need
         to create individual IAM users in each account

   Example Use Case
      you have:
         - development (dev) account
         - production (prod) account
      give access:
         - to developers in dev account access to one S3 bucket in production account
           containing historical production data

     Steps to configure:
       - in dev account, create Developers IAM Group (DevGroup)
       - in dev account, create 'Anna' user and add her to up Developers IAM Group (DevGroup)
       - in prod account, create S3 "my-prod_shared-data" bucket
       - in prod account, create IAM Policy, dev-read-write-bucket, with access to S3 my-prod-shared-data
         bucket and attach it to IAM Role, prodShareDataRole
          - this IAM Role, devS3AccessRole, it will reference account ID of the dev account
       - in dev account, attach IAM Inline policy to DevGroup that allows it to assume the prod account IAM prodSharedDataRoek role
            Note: does not need to be Inline policy
